**What R5 is, precisely.** R5 is a **narrow correction pass on R4**, not a redesign and not an implementation authorization. It does four things and nothing else: **(a)** it completes the **Gate 27 acceptance-criteria coverage** by adding, for every lifecycle-ending and environmental scenario, either (i) a criterion that asserts a **mechanism-independent observable outcome**, or (ii) an explicit **deferred** marker naming the requirement, the missing criterion and the owner decision that blocks it — and it never records a deferred requirement as covered (§15.8); **(b)** it re-verifies the **failed-asset-write** criterion mapping against the actual §15.1 rows (§3.5 `R5-02`) instead of trusting R4's sentence; **(c)** it re-measures **every source line citation that supports a current claim** and repairs the ones that had drifted (§3.5 `R5-03`, `R5-04`); and **(d)** it reconciles the **correction history, finding register and integrity table** so that no completeness statement is stronger than its evidence (§3.5 `R5-01`, `R5-05`). **R5 adds no gate, closes no gate, adds no phase, changes no ownership assignment, selects no concurrency mechanism and adds no capability.**
**What R4 is (history).** R4 is a **narrow correction pass, not a redesign**. Its honest starting point is that the two R3 reviews were run against the **pre-repair** R3 draft, and the repairs those reviews prompted were **already applied in R3 itself** before it was stamped (§7 of `DOCUMIND_PLAN_R3_CORRECTION_REPORT_2026-10-14.md` records them). R4 therefore does three things and nothing else: it **verifies** each reviewed finding against the current source and the finished R3 text; it **corrects the one item that is still wrong** (`R4-01`: the integrity row's Gate-27 section list omits §10.2) and the two items that were **only partially completed** (`R4-07`, `R4-08`); and it records the dispositions as `R4-01 … R4-08`, **plus one defect its own source re-read found and neither reviewer raised** (`R4-A1`), with the evidence for each (§3.4, §20 `R-53 … R-61`). It adds no capability, removes none, and changes no gate.
**Owner decision incorporated (2026-10-14):** reprocessing is **OPTION B — write first, commit the new structure, then clean up the old assets** (§10.5). The previous committed structure stays usable if a replacement run fails before its commit. **This authorizes documentation only:** no implementation, schema change, migration, dependency or production edit.
**Owner decision NOT made — Gate 27 (new in R3, extended by R4 and R5):** **per-document run claim, active-run identity, collision-resistant run identification and cleanup exclusion** (§10.6). The Option-B guarantee **requires** a defensible per-document concurrency mechanism that the current code does **not** have; R3/R4 state the required properties and record the decision instead of selecting a mechanism. **Gate 27 is not approved; Phase 2a remains blocked on it.** R4 adds to it the run-identifier requirements of `R4-08` (documented source of truth, lifetime/scope, uniqueness expectation, client-unmanipulability, and fail-closed collision behaviour) — **still as requirements, still without choosing an implementation.** **R5 adds the lifecycle-ending requirement set (§15.8): eight scenarios — worker/process death, container restart, request timeout, client disconnect, request termination after claim acquisition, acquisition/release/expiry/recovery when execution ends unexpectedly, retry after interruption, and a competing request while the previous run may still be active — each with a named criterion where the required behaviour is mechanism-independent, and each with an explicit `DEFERRED` marker where it is not. **Seven of the eight have at least one specified criterion; one (`S5`) has none; no scenario is recorded as fully covered, and none is recorded as covered merely because it is deferred (§15.8's coverage statement).**

## 1. Purpose and Scope of This Revision

**Purpose.** Correct the four HIGH findings and the fifteen remaining findings of the independent review **in the plan text**, without redesigning the application and without changing a single line of runtime behaviour. The original plan's architecture, Contracts A–F and Phases 0–7 are retained wherever they remain valid; every correction is targeted and traceable to a finding (see §20).

**Purpose of revision R2 (this document).** R2 inherits the purpose above and adds one narrower job: correct the thirteen findings (`F-1 … F-13`) raised by an **independent readiness audit of R1**, and implement the owner's **Option-B** reprocessing decision. Its corrections are `R2-01 … R2-06` (§3.2) together with the verification-driven corrections `V2R-1 … V2R-8` (§3.2 note, §20 R-39…R-45). Scope is unchanged: no contract is added or removed, no phase is added or split, no capability is promoted to "existing", and the nine-agent ownership layer, the phase sequence and every pre-existing gate are preserved.

**Purpose of revision R3 (this document).** R3 inherits both purposes above and adds one narrower job: correct the findings of the **R2 Final Concurrency and Verification Audit** (`A-1 … A-5`, `B-1 … B-7`) in the plan text, and stop the plan from claiming concurrency safety it cannot deliver. Its corrections are `K-01 … K-07` (§3.3). Scope is unchanged: no contract is added or removed, no phase is added or split, no capability is promoted to "existing", and the nine-agent ownership layer, the phase sequence and every pre-existing gate are preserved. **R3 adds exactly one gate (27) and removes claims, not capabilities.**

**What R3 changes, in one sentence each.**

1. The existing document-status guard is **not** an exclusive run claim; §7.3, §10.1, §10.5 and §15.1 now say so, and a **new Gate 27** requires the owner to choose the per-document claim mechanism before Phase 2a (K-01).
2. The Option-B asset cleanup **cannot** be called concurrency-safe without an active-run exclusion; §10.5's guarantee is narrowed to what the described mechanism actually delivers, and §10.6 states the exclusion as a **required, undelivered** property (K-02).
3. The plan's stale-run recovery path is described **as the code actually behaves**: `processing` is not a durably observable state in the current single-transaction lifecycle, so a crash rolls the whole transaction back and the stale path is **not** the recovery mechanism for a crash (K-03).
4. "No orphan growth" is replaced by **measurable** acceptance criteria that match a best-effort, approval-gated cleanup (K-04).
5. The test-readiness statement is corrected: the **unit suite runs against in-memory SQLite and needs no external service**; only database-dependent integration and browser E2E suites are infrastructure-bound (K-05 — see `DOCUMIND_PLAN_R3_VERIFICATION_REPORT_2026-10-14.md` §2).
6. Phase 1a's `partial` status and the frontend union are presented as an **explicit owner choice** (Option A: minimum coordinated frontend change in 1a; Option B: backend-only with a documented compatibility window), not silently decided (K-06).
7. Seven consistency defects (`B-3 … B-7` plus cross-document drift) are corrected at their root: figure-identity supersession, ownership-matrix attribution, phase-map gate consistency, atomicity wording and the `MIN_USABLE_CHARS` home (K-07).

**Purpose of revision R4 (this document).** R4 inherits every purpose above and adds one narrower job: dispose of the eight findings of the **two read-only reviews of R3** (`R4-01 … R4-08`, §3.4) by verifying each against the finished R3 text and the current source, completing the ones that were only partially done, and correcting the one that is still wrong. **R4 does not reopen any R3 decision.** The nine-agent ownership layer, the phase sequence, the Gate 26/Gate 27 separation, the "concurrency control is not delivered" status, the `partial`/frontend owner choice and every contract are preserved exactly.

**Purpose of revision R5 (this document).** R5 inherits every purpose above and adds one narrower job: **close the Gate 27 acceptance-criteria question** that R4 left as a named gap, and reconcile the correction history and citation evidence that the R4 review round flagged (§3.5). It (a) enumerates the eight lifecycle-ending and environmental scenarios and gives each either a criterion asserting a **mechanism-independent observable outcome** or an explicit **`DEFERRED`** marker — and counts the result honestly (**2 SPECIFIED / 5 PARTIAL / 1 DEFERRED with no criterion / 0 fully covered**, §15.8); (b) re-verifies the failed-asset-write mapping against the actual matrix; (c) re-measures **every** citation supporting a current claim; and (d) reconciles the finding register and integrity table. **R5 does not reopen any R3 or R4 decision, selects no concurrency mechanism, adds no gate and adds no capability.** The nine-agent ownership layer, the phase sequence, the Gate 26/Gate 27 separation, the "concurrency control is not delivered" status, the `partial`/frontend owner choice and every contract are preserved exactly.

**What R5 changes, in one sentence each.**

1. **`R5-01`** — Gate 27's lifecycle-ending and environmental scenarios are enumerated with per-scenario criteria where the behaviour is mechanism-independent and explicit `DEFERRED` markers where it is not; **a named gap is replaced by criteria plus open requirements, and nothing deferred is counted as covered**.
2. **`R5-02`** — the failed-asset-write criterion mapping is verified against §15.1 and attributed explicitly to **row 38**, not to any AC-row.
3. **`R5-03`** — every source citation supporting a current claim is re-measured from the checked-out source; all are exact, and one precision refinement was made.
4. **`R5-04`** — the accidentally duplicated front-matter identity block for R1 is removed, so each earlier revision is stated once.
5. **`R5-05`** — the correction history, finding register and integrity table are reconciled, with new R5 rows and a completeness claim scoped to what was actually checked.
6. **`R5-A1`** — §15.8 becomes the single place where a scenario's coverage status is assigned.
7. **`R5-A2`** — §10.6 properties 4 and 9 require per-scenario treatment instead of one "crashes" statement.
8. **`R5-A3`** — §19 gains the honest public wording for unexpected termination and release.

**What R4 changes, in one sentence each (history).**

1. **`R4-01`** — the integrity row's Gate-27 section list is **re-derived from the file** and corrected: it had omitted **§10.2**, which does contain `gate 27` (§10.2 item 7). A per-subsection listing of where the phrase actually occurs is now stated with the search form used, so the claim is independently checkable rather than asserted.
2. **`R4-02`** — verified: the obsolete `G3` identifier no longer appears as a live reference anywhere; §15.1 row 38 cites **`G1/G3′`**, and every other `G3` occurrence is either the split `G3′`/`G3″` or an explicitly historical "old G3"/"the former G3" mention. R4 records the audit that established this.
3. **`R4-03`** — the digest evidence is made auditable: the integrity row now names **§11 of the R4 verification report** as the single digest register, and that register is written as a **digest manifest** with explicit provenance labels (coordinator-measured), the convention per artifact, and the acyclic reference direction. The R3 wording that pointed at a non-existent `§20` companion statement is gone (§3.4, and R3's own repair is noted as history).
4. **`R4-04`** — verified: the `status="processing"` constructor assignment is cited as **`:438`** in §2.5 C-3 and §7.5; `:435` appears only inside the explicit "not `:435`, which is `file_size=len(file_bytes)`" correction note. R4 re-checks that no other citation of the same assignment inherits the wrong line.
5. **`R4-05`** — verified: §7.5 describes **three** materially distinct write paths (new upload, upload dedupe/requeue, explicit reprocess), each with its own commit site, and states the flushed-vs-durably-committed distinction. R4 adds nothing here beyond confirmation and the residue consequences in item 7 below.
6. **`R4-06`** — completed: cleanup-related states 5, 6, 7, 10 and 12 now each carry their conditions explicitly, and §10.5 adds a **required-properties checklist** for cleanup (sequential-or-coordinated execution, active-run exclusion, indeterminate-run exclusion, verified containment and file identity, and **no deletion when ownership or run state cannot be established**), together with an explicit statement that **cleanup is not implemented** and that specifying required behaviour is not a claim about current behaviour.
7. **`R4-07`** — completed: the initial-upload crash statement is bounded to the interval **before the transaction commits**, now states the filesystem residue and the reconciliation path that covers it, and is explicitly **distinguished from reprocessing**, where a previously committed document and status already exist. No crash was reproduced and none is claimed.
8. **`R4-08`** — completed: **Gate 27** gains a full run-identifier requirement set — server-generated, collision-resistant, with a documented source of truth, lifetime/scope, uniqueness expectation, client-unmanipulability, and **fail-closed behaviour on collision or duplication (never overwrite or delete another run's assets)** — plus acceptance criterion **AC-8**, row 48 of §15.1, and a §15.4 phrase rule. The research-layer run-ID convention is named as a **precedent only**, explicitly not importable without an ownership and dependency decision, and **no locking/lease/claim implementation is selected**.

**History versus current design.** §3.1, §4, §20's `R-01 … R-30` and the four R1 reports record **R1's** correction pass as history. Where any of them describes the figure-asset lifecycle — deterministic `(document_id, sequence_index)` names, the created-vs-overwritten cleanup rule, or the "bytes overwritten" limitation — **§10.5 as rewritten in R2 supersedes them** and is the only current statement of that lifecycle. §3.2, §20's `R-31 … R-45` and the two R2 reports record **R2's** correction pass as history; where any of them describes the asset lifecycle, the chunk exclusion, the reason-visibility contract, the rate-limit owner or the migration numbering, **§10.5/§10.6, §10.1 step 6b, §7.2.3, §13.1 C-1c and §13.1's numbering note remain current**, and R3 corrects only the concurrency, cleanup-safety, recovery-accuracy, acceptance-criteria, test-readiness, frontend-compatibility and consistency defects it names.

**Superseded statement — the retracted derived-`figureRef` design (B-3).** R1's **derived** figure reference — `figureRef` computed from the existing `(document_id, sequence_index)` columns and never stored — is **withdrawn** by R2 (§10.1 step 5, §10.5) and is **not** the current design. Two places in this document **restate** it and are now explicitly struck through and labelled as history: §3.1's correction-`C3` row and §20's `R-24`. A third place, §10.5, **mentions** it only to reject it ("a derived `figureRef` cannot identify the right one"), which is a discussion rather than a restatement. No other section may state a derivation rule. **The single authoritative statement of how a figure's asset identity is decided is §10.5's gate-26 resolution table**: `figureRef` may be emitted only when a persisted per-element identifier (`asset_ref`, gate 26 option (a)) or an asset table (option (b)) allows the resolver to prove *which run* wrote a committed figure's file; under option (c) **no `figureRef` is emitted at all**. No other section may state a competing derivation rule.

**In scope of this revision**

1. A disposition for every finding REV-001 … REV-019, each with rationale and evidence (§4).
2. A fully specified document lifecycle and status contract, including the reversal of a documented invariant (§7).
3. An explicit three-option decision for OCR provenance storage, with the evidence that makes one of the review's assumptions impossible (§8).
4. A safe, auditable, approval-gated policy for previously processed documents (§9).
5. A complete, implementable figure evidence path and an explicit equation decision (§10).
6. Model/answer-generation gates plus an honest unavailable-state behaviour (§11).
7. API/frontend compatibility rules that forbid nonfunctional controls (§12).
8. A file-level single-writer ownership matrix that respects the nine registered agents (§13).
9. Phases split so each deliverable is independently verifiable, with prerequisites, prohibited files, rollback and evidence (§14).
10. Measurable acceptance criteria and the full required-test inventory (§15).
11. Security, resource-control, idempotency and recovery requirements (§16), deployment/provisioning/rollback (§17).
12. The owner decisions that remain, each with options, evidence, recommended default and what stays blocked (§18).
13. An explicit list of deferred capabilities, described as unsupported (§19).

**Out of scope (hard boundary).** Implementing any phase; editing production or frontend code; schema or migration authoring; running the audit or any reprocessing; installing packages; downloading models; changing `.env`, auth, deployment or infrastructure; editing the original plan, the review report or the agent contracts; any destructive or state-mutating git operation.

**Two findings discovered by this revision's own re-verification** (not present in the review) are recorded in §2.4 and carried into §4 as `N-001` and `N-002`; both change the shape of an owner decision, and neither is presented as resolved.

**Three findings discovered by R3's own re-verification** are recorded in §2.5 and carried into §3.3 as `N-003` (the status guard is non-atomic **and** `processing` is not durably observable), `N-004` (the current in-process delete of derived rows makes a crash-consistent middle state impossible) and `N-005` (no run identity exists anywhere in the schema, so "cleanup scoped to the committed reference set" cannot in fact exclude an active run). None is presented as resolved; each is routed to Gate 27 or recorded as a limitation.

### 2.4 New facts discovered by this revision (not in the review)

**N-001 — `insights.pageCount` cannot serve as a coverage denominator.** `[V]` `documents/insights.py:95-102` computes `pageCount` as `int(max_page)` over `document_chunks`. A 5-page PDF whose last two pages were silently dropped reports `pageCount = 3` and `pagesRepresented = 3`, i.e. **it looks complete**. The true count exists only transiently at ingestion (`parser.py:269`, `:327-338`) and is **not persisted**. Consequence: any "document-level completeness" reporting built on today's insights would repeat the silent-loss defect in a new form. This sharpens REV-004 (§8) and adds a required field decision (§18 gate 23).

**N-002 — Deployment files have no registered owner.** `[V]` No agent contract in `agents/*.md` lists `backend/docker-entrypoint.sh`, `backend/Dockerfile`, `compose.yaml` or `Dockerfile.frontend` in its **Owns** list (checked: `backend-agent.md` owns `main.py`, `config.py`, `storage/file_store.py`, `auth/**`, `user/**`, `settings/**`, `verification/**`, `reliability/**`, `documents/**`, `requirements.txt`; `database-agent.md` owns `migrations/**`, `<feature>/models.py`, `storage/database.py`, `alembic.ini`, `scripts/**`). Therefore the original plan's §11/§13 assumption that "deployment changes" are simply a `BACKEND_AGENT` approval item is **not executable under the current ownership layer**. Deployment edits require a `COORDINATOR`-owned contract amendment approved by the user (the 1.6L/1.6L-style precedent in `agents/coordinator.md`). Not blocked, but explicitly gated (§18 gate 22).

### 2.5 Concurrency, transaction-lifecycle and filesystem facts re-verified by R3 `[V]`

Every row below was read directly from the source in this pass. These are the facts the R2 audit's `A-1 … A-3` rest on, and they are what §10.5/§10.6/§15.1 are now required to match.

| # | Fact re-verified | Evidence (file:line) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C-1 | **The status guard is a read-then-act check, not an atomic claim.** `reprocess_document` reads the row with a plain `select(Document).options(selectinload(Document.chunks))`, evaluates `doc.status == "processing" and not _is_stale_processing(doc)` in Python, and only *afterwards* sets the new status — all inside one session that commits at the end of the request | `documents/routes.py:672-678` (read), `:681-685` (guard + 409), `:711` (`_process_document` sets `processing`), `:713` (single commit) |
| C-2 | **No lock, no conditional update and no claim exists anywhere in application code.** Zero occurrences of `with_for_update` / `FOR UPDATE` / `pg_advisory*` / `advisory_lock`, and zero occurrences of the identifiers `run_id` and `asset_ref`, in application code (`auth`, `chat`, `documents`, `embeddings`, `brain`, `graph`, `storage`, `settings`, `verification`, `reliability`, `scripts`, `main.py`, `config.py`) — **both searches return 0**. **Scope note (added after review):** these are the two scoped searches actually run; a *bare* search for the substring `lease` was **not** run and would produce false positives ("release", "Please") — so this row claims only the two identifiers, not the word `lease`. **A run-identity convention does already exist in the research layer** (`[V]` `backend/evaluation/artifacts.py:77` `def new_run_id(now=None) -> str`), but `AGENTS.md` §03 forbids production importing from `evaluation/`, so it is a **precedent to cite** in Gate 27, not a mechanism available to Phase 2a | repo-wide scoped search + `evaluation/artifacts.py:77`, this pass |
| C-3 | **`processing` is not a durably observable state in the current lifecycle.** The upload path writes `status="processing"` in the row constructor (`[V]` `:438` — **not `:435`, which is `file_size=len(file_bytes)`; corrected after review in R3 and re-verified against the source in R4**), flushes once (`:445`) to obtain the id, runs the whole pipeline (`:452`) and commits **once** (`:456`) — by which time the pipeline has already set the final `ready`/`error`. `reprocess_document` behaves identically (delete derived rows → `_process_document` → **one** `await db.commit()` at `:713`), and the upload endpoint's **dedupe/requeue branch** commits at `:412` and `:420`. `get_db` adds a *second* commit only after the handler returns (`storage/database.py:60`) | `documents/routes.py:438-461`, `:412`, `:420`, `:696-713`; `storage/database.py:56-63` |
| C-4 | **A crash therefore rolls the whole transaction back.** A process killed at any point between `:445` and `:456` (upload) or `:696` and `:713` (reprocess) loses the entire transaction: the connection is closed/rolled back by `async_session`, PostgreSQL discards the uncommitted `UPDATE`s, and the **previously committed state survives intact**. No `processing` row is left behind, so **the stale-run path is not the recovery mechanism for a crash** | `documents/routes.py:445-461`, `:696-713`; `storage/database.py:56-63` |
| C-5 | **The stale-run path exists but is reachable only from a *committed* `processing` row** — which the code above never produces. `_is_stale_processing` returns `True` for `status == "processing"` with `processing_started_at is None` (`:325-326`) or older than `_STALE_PROCESSING_SECONDS = 600` (`:66`, `:327-328`); it is consulted at exactly two call sites, the dedupe requeue (`:415-416`) and the reprocess guard (`:681`) | `documents/routes.py:66`, `:321-328`, `:415-416`, `:681` |
| C-6 | **No run identity is persisted, so "the committed reference set" cannot exclude an active run.** Chunk rows carry no uniqueness at all beyond their primary key, and the only uniqueness constraints in the affected tables are `document_table_rows(document_id, table_element_id, row_index)` (`documents/models.py:170-175`) and `document_structure_elements(document_id, sequence_index)` (`graph/models.py:202-205`) — both of which a *replace* run reuses rather than excludes | `documents/models.py:165-175`, `graph/models.py:198-205` |
| C-7 | **Derived rows are deleted in-process, inside the same uncommitted transaction, before the replacement is built** — so under today's code there is no instant at which a *new* structure exists while the *old* one is still committed, and no instant at which the old rows are gone while the new ones are not yet written | `documents/routes.py:696-705` (chunk loop `:696-697`, up-front `delete(DocumentStructureElement)` `:701-705`, statement on `:702`), `:708` (flush), `:711` (pipeline) |
| C-8 | **The filesystem is not in that transaction.** Files are written before the commit and removed best-effort after it (`delete_document` commits the DB first at `:629` and then removes the file), and `cleanup_new_file` (`storage/file_store.py:201-214`) is a rollback helper, not a transaction. Nothing in the codebase coordinates a DB commit and a filesystem state change atomically | `documents/routes.py:425-461`, `:612-634`; `storage/file_store.py:201-214` |
| C-9 | **The test suite's default engine is in-memory SQLite, not PostgreSQL** — so a large part of the suite is runnable with no external service | `tests/conftest.py:50` (`sqlite+aiosqlite:///:memory:`), `:147-149` (`poolclass=StaticPool`), `:154-156` (`PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`) and `:1-9` (module docstring — **the previously cited range stopped at `:50` and did not contain the `StaticPool` it named; corrected in R4, `R4-A1`**), `pytest.ini:11` (`testpaths = tests` — **line 11, corrected after review**; line 9 is a comment) |

**New findings from this pass (not in the R2 audit), each labelled and routed, not resolved.**

**N-003 — the guard's non-atomicity and `processing`'s non-observability are two different defects with one fix.** `[V]` rows C-1 and C-3 together mean (i) two concurrent reprocess requests can both read the same committed `ready`/`partial`/`error` status and both pass the guard, and (ii) *no* crash can leave a stale `processing` row for the current recovery path to find. The first is a concurrency defect; the second is a **recovery-path reachability** defect. Fixing the first by committing a claim *creates a durable `processing` state for the first time*, which in turn **makes the stale path live** and gives `processing_started_at is None ⇒ stale` real consequences (a claim written without its timestamp would be superseded immediately by any concurrent run). Both therefore belong to **Gate 27** and are stated there (§10.6).

**N-004 — §10.5's "write → commit → clean up" ordering cannot be implemented by the current in-process delete.** `[V]` row C-7: the derived rows are removed with `await db.delete(...)` / `delete(DocumentStructureElement)` on the *same session* that later commits, so the delete and the insert are one transaction and there is no observable window in which the committed old structure has gone. R2 correctly marks that ordering as *current behaviour only*, but it never names the consequence: **the Option-B replacement requires the delete to move to the post-commit sweep** (or the old rows to be superseded rather than deleted up front), which is a change to `reprocess_document`'s transaction shape — i.e. exactly the change Gate 27 governs. §14.2 Phase 2a and §10.5 now say so explicitly.

**N-005 — "cleanup scoped to the committed reference set" does not, by itself, exclude an active run.** `[V]` row C-6 plus the asset-naming rule: candidate files from a *concurrent* run R are, by construction, referenced by *no* committed element until R commits; so a sweep that asks only "is this file referenced by the currently committed structure?" answers *no* for R's candidates and would delete them — the exact A-2 failure. Run-scoped names prevent *overwriting* referenced bytes (G2) but say nothing about *deleting* another run's not-yet-referenced bytes. The exclusion needs run **state**, not naming. This is stated as a required, undelivered property in §10.6 and as a narrowed guarantee (G3′) in §10.5.

### 3.1 Correction pass R1 — the four defects corrected in this revision

These were found by inspection of R0 against the source, not reported by the review. Each is corrected **everywhere it occurs**, not only at its first appearance (§20 lists the entries). **History note (added in R3):** this table records how R1 corrected R0 and is retained unchanged for traceability. Two of its clauses were themselves corrected later and must not be read as current design: `C3`'s **derived `figureRef`** clause (superseded by R2 — see the R3 note on that row) and `C4`'s asset-lifecycle clauses, which **§10.5/§10.6** (R2, corrected in R3) now supersede.

| # | Defect in R0 | Correction made in R1 | Affected sections |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **C1** | **Status contradiction.** R0's Phase 1a test list and the §15.1 acceptance table said a scanned-only PDF becomes `partial` in Phase 1a — but a scanned-only PDF has **zero** usable pages, and R0's own definitions make zero usable pages `error`. Two sections therefore asserted different verdicts for the same document. | One authoritative verdict function is now stated once (§7.2.1) and every other statement refers to it: `pages_usable == 0` ⇒ **`error`** (never `partial`), `1 <= pages_usable < pages_total` ⇒ `partial`, `pages_usable == pages_total` ⇒ `ready`, any raised step ⇒ `error`; a scanned-only document that OCRs fully becomes **`ready`**. `partial` vs `error` is preserved exactly: `error` requires *zero* usable pages. | §5, §7.2-§7.2.2, §7.5, §7.6, §14.2 (1a, 1b), §15.1, §15.2, §19 |
| **C2** | **OCR failure conflated with `partial`.** R0 stated in three places that an OCR failure ⇒ `partial` (verdict bullet, Phase 1b failure rule, §15.1 row), which is wrong when nothing else is usable and would let an entirely unreadable document be reported as "success with disclosure". | OCR failure is now a **coverage** event, not a verdict: verdict per §7.2.1. All-pages OCR failure ⇒ `error`/`ocr_failed_all_pages`; one-page failure with usable pages elsewhere ⇒ `partial`; OCR output below the usability minimum counts as an unaccounted page; raised runner ⇒ `error`/`pipeline_exception`. New test scenario added — **§15.1 row 32** (all-pages OCR failure) — plus the **rewritten row 31** (sub-minimum OCR output now counts as an *unaccounted* page; in R0 that row said the page was accounted for and OCR-derived). Rows 33-34 belong to correction C4, not C2 (attribution corrected in R2, F-11). | §7.5, §14.2 (1b), §15.1, §15.2 |
| **C3** | **Unstoreable persisted value.** R0 said a figure is persisted with `figureRef=None` *and a reason* when asset extraction fails, while also declaring "no schema change". `[V]` `document_structure_elements` has **no** column for a failure reason (and `content` is reserved for the verbatim caption); the claim was unstoreable. | The design no longer persists a failure: the figure **row is created only after its asset was written** (persist-last), ~~`figureRef` is a **derived** value from the existing `(document_id, sequence_index)` columns (never stored)~~ **[SUPERSEDED — history only (B-3, K-07).]** the derived-`figureRef` clause in this row was **withdrawn by R2** and is **not** current design: under run-scoped asset names a derived reference cannot identify *which run's* file belongs to a committed element. **§10.5's gate-26 resolution table is the single authoritative statement of asset identity**; `figureRef` is emitted only if a persisted identifier (`asset_ref`) or an asset table is approved, and not at all under the no-DDL fallback. The surviving parts of this correction remain current: no failure reason is stored, and an asset-write failure produces **no figure row** plus a **reported** (logged / ingestion-summary) diagnostic — with durable figure provenance explicitly routed to an owner decision (gate 25). No schema field was added and no migration was authorized. | §10.1 (steps 3/5, failure matrix), §10.5, §14.2 (2a), §16.2, §18 |
| **C4** | **Asset lifecycle assumed atomicity.** R0 described asset write + row persistence, deletion and reprocessing without stating ordering, partial-failure recovery, orphan handling, or the fact that `[V]` `delete_upload` **refuses non-regular files** (`storage/file_store.py:188-192`, `stat.S_ISREG`) and the project's own delete path is DB-first with **best-effort** file removal. | A complete, non-atomic-by-design lifecycle is specified in §10.5: file-first/DB-last ordering; defensive per-run cleanup **of the files that run created (never of a path that already existed)**; interrupted-run residue; per-file deletion via the **existing audited** primitive plus a new containment-checked listing helper; orphan reconciliation under approval; cross-tenant resolution impossible by construction; and the residual risk (an interrupted replace can leave an asset whose committed element came from the previous run) **stated as a limitation** with its own owner decision (gate 25). | §10.1, §10.5, §14.2 (2a), §15.1, §16.2, §18 |

**Identifier note:** the correction items above are labelled `C1`-`C4` (this revision's correction pass); they are a different namespace from the ownership-matrix rows `C-1a`-`C-18` in §13.1 (`C-4`, the withdrawn equations row, is not related to correction `C4`). Cross-references in this document always use the hyphenated form for matrix rows.

**C1/C2 semantics are compatible with the original plan's status definitions** (`uploaded`/`processing`/`ready`/`error` existed; `partial` is the revision's addition) and with every owner gate: no gate is closed or altered by this pass.

### 3.2 Correction pass R2 — the independent readiness audit's findings, corrected in this revision

An **independent readiness audit of R1** (two read-only reviewers plus coordinator shell measurements; the audit is summarised in `DOCUMIND_PLAN_R2_CORRECTION_REPORT_2026-10-14.md`) raised thirteen findings, `F-1 … F-13` (2 HIGH, 3 MEDIUM, 6 LOW, 2 INFORMATIONAL). R2 corrects them as follows. Each is corrected **everywhere it occurs**; §20 lists the entries (R-31 … R-38).

| # | Audit finding(s) | Defect corrected in R2 | Where | Owner approval needed? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **R2-01** | **F-1 (HIGH)** | **Figure elements must not enter the ordinary text chunk/embed stream.** R1 added `figure` to the extractor vocabulary and parser maps but never excluded it from `chunk_text`, which emits one chunk per element (`[V]` `documents/routes.py:125,198`); a caption-bearing figure would therefore produce a **second** caption chunk and a caption-less figure an **empty** chunk, while Phase 3b's acceptance demanded "exactly one chunk contains the caption text". R2 adds the chunk-path exclusion as an explicit requirement (§10.2 item 6), states the `DocumentChunk.element_type` behaviour for **both** chunkers, forbids any "chunked exactly once" claim unless the data flow guarantees it, and adds five acceptance rows (§15.1 rows 35-39). | §5, §6, §10.1 (steps 3, 6b, failure matrix), §10.2 (item 6), §14.2 (2a, 3b), §15.1 | no |
| **R2-02** | **F-2 (HIGH)** + **owner decision: Option B** | **Reprocessing is write-first → commit → clean up, with a run-scoped asset set.** R1's §10.5 asserted this ordering as normative while the code the plan itself cites deletes derived rows **up front** (`[V]` `:696-705`, cited in R2 as `:695-703` and re-measured in R4), so the stated residue ("previous rows keep their slots with newer bytes") could not occur and the reconciling edit was unassigned. R2 states the design for all twelve required states, replaces deterministic document+sequence asset names with **run-scoped immutable** names, scopes cleanup to the **committed reference set**, defines crash/retry/orphan/corruption/concurrency behaviour, marks the delete-first behaviour as **current only**, assigns the edit to Phase 2a / `BACKEND_AGENT`, and adds **gate 26** for the one piece of persistence the guarantee needs (an asset identifier per element, or an asset table). | §6, §7.5, §9.2, §10.2 (item 7), **§10.5 (rewritten)**, §13.1 (C-3, C-3b), §14.2 (2a), §15.1 (rows 23, 34, 38, 39), §16.2, §18 (gate 26) | **yes** — gate 26 (additive column/table) plus gate 5 |
| **R2-03** | **F-3 (MEDIUM)** | **The deterministic asset-write contract is specified.** R1 defined read/serve, listing and deletion safety but never the write side, and left `<ext>` undefined. R2 fixes the format and extension **server-side** (never from document content), defines path derivation and containment, exclusive creation, temp-then-rename completeness, collision refusal, run association, the refusal to replace a referenced asset, and which primitives are reused versus extended. | §10.5 (write contract), §13.1 (C-3), §14.2 (2a), §16.4, §19 | no |
| **R2-04** | **F-4 (MEDIUM)** | **Status-reason visibility is a contract, not an assumption.** R1 said the reason is "recorded … at document level" while no option (B/C) can store one. R2 adds a **reason-visibility contract** distinguishing transient (ingestion response), logged, durably persisted, and later-API-visible; states explicitly that under options B/C the reason is **transient and not retrievable later**; keeps any durable reason behind gates 3/23; and restricts Phase 1a's acceptance criteria to what the approved storage contract can deliver. | §7.2 (new §7.2.3), §7.4, §8.4, §12.2, §14.2 (1a), §15.1 | only if a field/table route is chosen (gates 3/23) |
| **R2-05** | **F-5 (MEDIUM)** | **The rate-limit change has an owner, a file, a phase and a gate.** R1 required middleware in `backend/main.py` (§16.1) and made it an acceptance criterion (§15.1 row 22) while no matrix row and no 1b/4 phase file list contained that file (only C-10, Phase 5b, did). R2 adds matrix row **C-1c** (`BACKEND_AGENT`, `backend/main.py`, gate 21), lists the file in Phase 1b/4, and adds the missing multi-touch rows to §13.3 (`backend/main.py`, `backend/brain/adapters.py`). | §13.1 (C-1c), §13.3, §14.2 (1b, 4), §15.1 row 22, §16.1 | value only (gate 21) |
| **R2-06** | **F-6 … F-13 (LOW/INFORMATIONAL)** | Eight minimal consistency corrections: (a) `no_text_layer` **removed** from the accepted reason set as redundant, with `ocr_failed_all_pages` defined to include all-pages sub-minimum output; (b) a per-page OCR **timeout** or resource failure explicitly classified as a **page-coverage event**, not a pipeline exception, unless the runner itself raises; (c) §15.1 rows 25-26's phase references corrected and row 26 marked `[B]`; (d) §14.1's map redrawn so 2a/2b hang off 1a as the hard-edge text already stated; (e) §13.3's omissions fixed; (f) the change-log/report attribution for rows 31-34 corrected; (g) the delete-first line references tightened (`:695-703` — **an R2-era range, retained here as history; re-measured in R4 as `:696-705`, see `R-61`**); (h) the R0 digest attribution corrected with **both** conventions named. | §7.2.1, §7.5, §13.3, §14.1, §14.2, §15.1, §20, integrity table | no |

**Identifier note (unchanged):** `R2-01 … R2-06` are this revision's correction items; `C1-C4` are R1's correction items; `C-1a … C-18` are ownership-matrix rows. The three are different namespaces and are never interchanged.

**Verification-driven corrections (`V2R-1 … V2R-8`), all corrected here.** After the R2-01…R2-06 pass, a **separate read-only reviewer** (file access, no shell) inspected this file against the source and raised eight findings: **V2R-1 (HIGH)** — the chunk exclusion was specified at the wrong point for the `structure-v2` path, where `_merge_class` merges any non-table/non-list type as *prose* and the chunk type is computed over the merged group, so filtering the output object would have been too late; **V2R-2** — three matrix rows claimed migration `008`; **V2R-3** — the rate-limit file was still absent from Phase 1b/4's file lists and gate 21 from Phase 1b's gate list although the change log claimed otherwise; **V2R-4** — the consolidated gate paragraph and the closing line still described the pre-R2 gate set; **V2R-5** — R1-era text in §1/§3/§3.1 and the "five edits" counts read as current design; **V2R-6** — five asset-citation statements were unconditional although gate 26 allows a no-DDL fallback; **V2R-7** — the §16.2 idempotency wording contradicted §9.2/§15.1; **V2R-8** — one mis-citation. Each is corrected in this revision (§20 R-39…R-45) and recorded in `DOCUMIND_PLAN_R2_VERIFICATION_REPORT_2026-10-14.md`.

**No gate is closed, opened or altered by this pass except the addition of gate 26**, which is a *new* decision the corrected design requires because the Option-B guarantee needs one piece of persistence the current schema lacks.

### 3.3 Correction pass R3 — the R2 Final Concurrency and Verification Audit's findings, corrected in this revision

The **R2 Final Concurrency and Verification Audit** (read-only; delivered in the R2 conversation, no file created) raised findings `A-1 … A-5` and `B-1 … B-7`. R3 corrects them as follows. Each is corrected **everywhere it occurs**; §20 lists the entries (R-46 … R-52).

| # | Audit finding(s) | Defect corrected in R3 | Where | Owner approval needed? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **K-01** | **A-1 (HIGH)** | **The status guard is not an exclusive run claim, and the plan said it was.** R2 stated that the guard "refuses a second non-stale run" and treated that as the concurrency mechanism (§10.5 state 12, "Concurrency, retry and idempotency"). `[V]` the guard is a read-then-act check on a snapshot (`:672-681`) with no lock, no conditional update and no constraint, and the new status is committed only at `:713` — so two requests can both pass it. R3 (a) states the distinction between *status check*, *atomic claim*, *committed run/lease record*, *stale-run recovery*, *successful commit* and *post-commit cleanup*; (b) withdraws every claim that the guard alone prevents concurrent processing; (c) states that the Option-B lifecycle **requires** a defensible per-document claim; (d) adds **Gate 27** (per-document run claim and concurrency control) with the required properties and the owner decision; (e) requires explicit handling of eleven concurrency/failure cases and fifteen proposed acceptance criteria (§15.6). **No mechanism is selected.** | §7.3, §7.5, §9.2, §10.1 (failure matrix), §10.5 (state 12, concurrency, limitations), **§10.6 (new)**, §13.1 (C-3), §14.2 (2a), §15.1 (rows 39, 40-47), **§15.6 (new)**, §16.2, §18 (gate 27) | **yes** — gate 27 + gate 5 |
| **K-02** | **A-2 (HIGH)** | **The cleanup sweep could delete an active run's candidate assets.** R2's predicate was "unreferenced by the currently committed structure" and its limitation row called the resulting loss "churn, not corruption of the committed set". `[V]` N-005: a concurrent run's candidates are referenced by nothing until that run commits, so the predicate matches them and a sweep would delete them — after which the other run commits a structure referencing deleted files. R3 (a) drops the "churn" framing; (b) requires an **active-run exclusion** mechanism before any concurrency-safety claim; (c) evaluates four design options without choosing one; (d) forbids relying on run-scoped filenames alone; (e) forbids relying on "re-read the committed reference set immediately before deletion"; (f) defines eligibility and the six asset classes; (g) makes the ambiguous case **delete nothing**; (h) states the relationship to Gate 27; (i) adds seven proposed concurrency acceptance criteria (§15.7). | §6 (invariants), **§10.5 (cleanup procedure, guarantees G3′, G9, limitations)**, **§10.6 (new)**, §14.2 (2a), §15.1 (rows 23, 34, 39; new rows 40-47), **§15.7 (new)**, §16.2, §19 | **yes** — gate 27 |
| **K-03** | **A-3 (MEDIUM), A-5 (informational)** | **The stale-run/recovery story did not match the code.** R2 described crash recovery through the existing stale-run path (state 5, §15.1 row 33) and repeated it in §7.5 and §16.2. `[V]` rows C-3…C-5 and C-7: the upload and reprocess runs are each **one** transaction, `processing` is never durably committed, a crash rolls everything back and leaves the **previous committed state intact**, and the pipeline's own exception handling already writes `ready`/`error` before the single commit. So a crash during initial upload leaves **no document row at all** (the file is the orphan, which `cleanup_new_file` and the approved orphan reconciliation already cover), and a crash during reprocess leaves the **previous** status and rows. R3 (a) traces both transaction boundaries explicitly; (b) distinguishes an uncommitted `processing` state from a persisted one; (c) states that the stale path is reachable only from a *committed* `processing` row, which today's code never produces; (d) documents `processing_started_at is None ⇒ stale` and its consequence for any future claim/lease; (e) requires the future claim write to be **committed and timestamped** if Gate 27 selects a lease; (f) invents no recovery mechanism. | **§7.5**, §9.2, **§10.5 (states 1, 4, 5)**, **§10.6**, §15.1 (rows 33, 39, 41, 43), §15.6, §16.2, §18 (gate 27) | no (documentation); **yes** for any mechanism (gate 27) |
| **K-04** | **A-4 (MEDIUM)** | **"No orphan growth" was unimplementable next to a best-effort, approval-gated cleanup.** R2 required it in §9.2, §16.2 and §15.1 rows 23/34 while simultaneously stating that cleanup is best-effort and reconciliation approval-gated. R3 replaces it with measurable criteria that the described design can actually meet: exactly one authoritative committed structure after a successful run; no committed element referencing a file deleted by cleanup; no deletion of an active or potentially-committing run's assets; failed/interrupted cleanup leaves **identifiable, bounded and reportable** residue; residue is reconcilable only through an explicitly authorised process; cleanup failures are observable and retryable. **Zero orphans is not promised.** | §9.2, §16.2, **§15.1 (rows 23, 34)**, **§15.5 (new criteria table)**, §10.5 (G8) | no (owner retains cleanup policy) |
| **K-05** | **B-1 (MEDIUM)** | **The R2 verification report's test-readiness row was overbroad.** It recorded "Running any test suite — BLOCKED … the suites need a live database". `[V]` C-9: the suite's default engine is in-memory SQLite and the unit suite was executed successfully in the prior audit (1,345 passed / 23 skipped / exit 0 / ≈67.6 s) with no external service. R3 (a) corrects the statement in the **R3** verification report; (b) separates *previously executed* unit results, tests not run in the R2 pass, tests not run in this R3 pass, PostgreSQL-dependent integration tests and browser E2E tests needing a live app; (c) presents the historical unit result as **prior evidence, not a result of this pass**; (d) states which checks are runnable without external infrastructure, from repository evidence; (e) claims nothing about integration/E2E. | **`DOCUMIND_PLAN_R3_VERIFICATION_REPORT_2026-10-14.md` §2/§5**, §2.5 (C-9), §15.5, §19 | no |
| **K-06** | **B-2 (MEDIUM)** | **`partial` versus the frontend union was an unowned decision.** R2 added `partial` in Phase 1a and required the frontend union/mapper change in the same coordinated change, while Phase 1a's own *Must not edit* list forbids `src/**`. R3 presents the owner with **Option A** (include the minimum coordinated frontend change in Phase 1a, with frontend ownership and tests) and **Option B** (backend-only 1a with an explicitly documented and accepted compatibility window plus the Phase 6 follow-up), states the user-visible behaviour of each, and **chooses neither**. It also records what the *existing* cast does with an unknown value in the interim (`apiDocToFile` casts without validating) so Option B's window is described honestly. | **§12.2**, **§14.2 (1a)**, §15.1 (row 24), §15.5, §18 (gate 4), §19 | **yes** — the choice rides gate 4 |
| **K-07** | **B-3 … B-7 (consistency)** | Five consistency corrections, each at its root: **(B-3)** R1's derived-`figureRef` design is labelled superseded wherever it survives (§1 history note, §3.1 C3 row, §20 R-24) and §10.5's gate-26 table is named the **single authoritative** statement of asset identity; **(B-4)** the ownership-matrix attribution is corrected — the page-record model belongs to `backend/documents/models.py` (C-2/C-2b), and the `backend/graph/models.py` single-writer row now lists **C-3b only**; **(B-5)** the §14.1 phase map is reconciled with §14.2 for **every** phase (Gate 16 for 1b; Gate 26's *conditional* relevance to 2a/3b/6; Gate 22 for 5b; Gate 21 for 7); **(B-6)** every "replaces rows atomically" claim is qualified as a **database-transaction** guarantee that never covers filesystem writes or cleanup; **(B-7)** the proposed home and owner of `MIN_USABLE_CHARS` are stated, or explicitly routed to an owner decision, with no implicit configuration change. | §1, §3.1, §6, **§7.5, §9.2, §10.2 (item 7)**, §12.2, **§13.1 (C-2/C-2b/C-3/C-3b), §13.3**, **§14.1**, §14.2, §15.4, §16.2, §18 (gates 24/27), §20, §2.5 | no (except the `MIN_USABLE_CHARS` home if it lands in a new config field — gate 24) |

**Identifier note (extended):** `K-01 … K-07` are R3's correction items; `R2-01 … R2-06` and `V2R-1 … V2R-8` are R2's; `C1-C4` are R1's; `C-1a … C-18` are ownership-matrix rows. The four are different namespaces and are never interchanged.

**What R3 does *not* do.** It does not select a claim mechanism (K-01), does not select a cleanup-exclusion mechanism (K-02), does not implement or design a recovery mechanism the code lacks (K-03), does not promise zero orphans (K-04), does not run a test suite (K-05), does not choose the frontend option (K-06) and does not add, remove or renumber any gate other than adding **27**.

**Gate status after R3.** Gates 1-26 are **unchanged** (none closed, none opened, none altered). Gate **27** is **added and not approved**. Gate 26 remains an **asset-identity** decision and does **not** absorb concurrency control — a point R3 states explicitly in §10.6 and §18, because it would be easy to read R2's gate 26 as "the asset question" and miss that the guarantee needs Gate 27 as well. **Phase 2a remains blocked until Gate 27 is decided and the resulting design is approved.**
