## 9. Existing-Document Audit and Reprocessing Policy (REV-003)

**Problem restated with evidence.** `[V]` today a mixed PDF can reach `ready` with pages absent (audit finding 2, re-confirmed in the original plan §1.2). An OCR feature added later does not retroactively fix documents already stored. Nothing in the original plan addressed those rows.

### 9.1 Detection strategy (read-only, auditable)

`[P]` a **read-only audit** — never a mutation — executed only after gate 17:

1. **Coverage shortfall:** for each PDF document, compare the source PDF's page count (recomputed from the stored file, read-only) against the distinct pages represented in `document_chunks` and `document_structure_elements`.
2. **Zero-content documents:** documents whose `chunk_count == 0` while `status == 'ready'` (a state the new contract forbids).
3. **Already-flagged documents:** any document already `partial` under the new contract, for reconciliation.
4. **Output:** a report (document ids, name, page counts, missing page numbers, current status, created_at) — counters and ids only, **never** extracted document text.

Ownership: the audit tool is a **script** → `DATABASE_AGENT` (`[V]` `agents/database-agent.md` Owns `backend/scripts/**`), named `[P]` `backend/scripts/audit_page_coverage.py`, **dry-run by default**, with no write path at all (its only mode is reporting). Any future write-capable variant is a separate, separately-approved artifact.

### 9.2 Eligibility and idempotency

* **Eligible** for reprocessing only after OCR is available (otherwise reprocessing cannot improve coverage) and only on explicit approval; not eligible while `processing`.
* **Identity preserved:** document `id`, `name`, `content_hash`, `file_type`, `created_at`, and the stored file are unchanged by reprocessing (`[V]` `documents/routes.py:664` already states identity/hash/file preservation).
* **Duplicate prevention.** `[V]` **today** reprocessing deletes derived rows before rebuilding — the chunk loop `:696-697` and the up-front `delete(DocumentStructureElement)` `:701-705` — and `structure.py` re-inserts inside a savepoint; row uniqueness `(document_id, table_element_id, row_index)` makes duplicate row evidence impossible; chunks are rebuilt with fresh indices. **The design replaces the up-front delete with the Option-B ordering (write → commit → clean up, §10.5):** the old rows are superseded *by* the committed replacement, not deleted before it. `[P]` the same replace-on-commit discipline is required for page records, and figure assets follow §10.5 (run-scoped names, reference-scoped cleanup), so **a single sequential retry** cannot double-write and cannot leave two live asset sets for the same committed structure.
* **A sequential retry is what that argument covers — not two concurrent runs (K-01, A-1).** `[V]` the delete and the insert in today's code share one transaction (`:696-705` then `:711-713`), so the replace-on-commit discipline **requires** the delete to move into the post-commit sweep — a transaction-shape change, not a statement reorder (§2.5 N-004). And **nothing in §9.2 arbitrates two runs at once**: the dedupe probe (`:397-402`) and the reprocess guard (`:681`) are both snapshot reads with no lock or constraint behind them, and `content_hash` has no unique index, so two concurrent uploads of the same content can both proceed. Sequential idempotency and concurrent exclusivity are **different properties**; only the first is argued here, and the second is **Gate 27** (§10.6).
* **Idempotency of a retry (R2-02, corrected in R3 by K-04).** A retry is a **new run** with a new run identifier: derived **rows** after a successful retry are identical to those of any other successful run of the same source; asset **filenames** differ by design (run-scoped, §10.5), which is why the acceptance criterion is stated as *identical derived rows and exactly one live asset set* rather than "identical filenames". **"With no orphan growth" has been removed as an acceptance criterion** (A-4): the design's cleanup is best-effort and its reconciliation is approval-gated, so zero-orphan growth is **not** a property this mechanism can guarantee, and stating it would have been an unsupported claim. The measurable criteria that replace it are §15.5's, and the acceptance rows are §15.1 rows 23 and 34. `[P]` idempotency of a **sequential** retry is argued by run identity; idempotency of **concurrent** runs is not argued at all — that is Gate 27.
* **Citations survive by construction:** `[V]` a source carries `chunkId` **and** the document/page identity (`chat/routes.py:40,43,45`) — the `chunkId` of a rebuilt chunk necessarily changes, while the **document + page** identity survives, which is why stored answer provenance is not invalidated by reprocessing.
* **Failure recovery:** a failed reprocess leaves `error` with the existing error semantics; the operator may retry; no partially rebuilt document is ever left `ready`.

### 9.3 Operational rollout (proposed, gated)

1. Owner approves the policy and the scope (gate 17).
2. Run the audit (§9.1) and record the report.
3. Owner reviews the report and approves a **bounded batch** (a named document-id list or an explicit count ceiling per run).
4. Run reprocessing through the **existing** route — no direct SQL writes, ever; status is written only by the pipeline.
5. Record before/after coverage per document; any document that cannot be improved is reported, not silently retried.

**Status rewrite is not part of the policy**: no script may set `status`; only the pipeline may.

---

## 10. Figure and Equation Representation and Retrieval (REV-001, REV-006, REV-007)

### 10.1 Figure evidence: complete end-to-end path (REV-001)

**Why the original plan was insufficient — `[V]`:** element-level structural candidates are seeded by **section-name matches only** and any element with empty content is **dropped** (`chat/rag.py:988-1071`); images cannot be embedded (the only embedding model is text, 384-dim); and the evidence contract has no asset field (`chat/routes.py:34-78`). Declaring a `figure` evidence kind therefore retrieves nothing.

**Proposed path (all `[P]`):**

| Step | Specification | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Detection | Enumerate image blocks that the extractor currently skips (`[V]` `documents/processing.py:249`) using the already-pinned PyMuPDF (`page.get_images()` / `get_pixmap()`); each becomes a candidate figure with page + bbox (PyMuPDF boxes are already collected for text) | BACKEND_AGENT |
| 2. Caption association | Deterministic geometric rule: the nearest text block whose box lies below the figure within a declared gap, else above, else **no caption**. The associated caption is stored **verbatim** as the figure element's `content`. Association is recorded, never inferred, and never invented if absent | BACKEND_AGENT |
| 3. Persistence | One `figure` element in the **existing** `document_structure_elements` row shape: `element_type='figure'`, `page_number`, `content` = caption text (or `''` when none), `bbox_x0..y1`, `parser_version`. Persisted by the existing writer (`[V]` `documents/structure.py:79`) — **no new column, no schema change** for figures. **Caption authority rule:** the figure's `content` is an **association copy only**; the caption's own paragraph element remains the **only** chunked/embedded copy (step 6), so the caption text is indexed exactly once. `[V]` requires the bbox data path of §10.2 items 4-5, because element bboxes are currently always `None`. **Persistence ordering (C3, restated by R2-02): a figure row is written only *after* its asset file has been written successfully in the same run** — so a persisted figure element always had an asset written for it by the run that created it. The asset's identity is **run-scoped** (§10.5) and its citation is enabled only once gate 26 is decided. No "failure reason" ever needs a column | BACKEND_AGENT |
| 4. Asset storage | The extracted image is written as a **run-scoped regular file** in the **canonical per-user asset location defined once in §10.5** (per-user upload subtree; name `<document_id>__<run_id>__<figure_sequence_index>.<ext>`; format and extension fixed server-side). §10.5 is the single statement of the layout and of the **write contract**, so no second description can drift from it; no ad-hoc path joining (reuse `storage/file_store.py` containment) | BACKEND_AGENT |
| 5. Asset reference | `figureRef = "fig:<asset_ref>"` — opaque, server-resolved, **never** a filesystem path or URL, resolved server-side with an ownership check — where `asset_ref` is the persisted per-element asset identifier. **This requires gate 26**: `asset_ref` has no column today, so under the no-DDL fallback **no `figureRef` is emitted and no asset route is enabled** (figures are then cited by caption/structure only, never with a guessed image). R1's derived-from-`(document_id, sequence_index)` form is **withdrawn** (R2-02): it cannot distinguish two runs for the same slot. **This step and §10.5's gate-26 table are the only authoritative statements of how asset identity is decided (B-3)**; no other section may state a derivation rule, and in particular no section may describe a `figureRef` that is derived from the element's position | BACKEND_AGENT |
| 5b. Asset read/serve | `[V]` `storage/file_store.py` has **no** read/serve primitive (`save_upload` `:31`, `delete_upload` `:137`, `cleanup_new_file` `:201`; containment helpers `_is_link_or_reparse_point` `:80`, `_resolved_within` `:97`). A **new** containment-checked read helper must be added there (reusing those helpers, never ad-hoc path joining) and consumed by an ownership-checked asset route | BACKEND_AGENT |
| 6. Retrieval path 1 — caption-mediated semantic | The caption is ordinary document text, so it is already chunked and embedded by the existing pipeline — and, **because of the exclusion in step 6b**, it is the **only** chunked copy. When a retrieved chunk's content contains a figure's caption (exact normalised match, non-empty) in the same document and page, the retriever **attaches** that figure (`caption`, `elementId`, and `figureRef` **only if gate 26 approves it**) to the returned source. No new index, no image embedding, no double-counting | AI_AGENT |
| **6b. Chunk/embed exclusion (R2-01 — must land with step 3, same phase, same owner)** | A figure element is **never** turned into a `DocumentChunk`. (a) **Legacy path:** the chunk list is built directly from extractor output (`[V]` `documents/routes.py:198` `extract_text` → `:125` `enumerate(chunk_text(elements))`) and `chunk_text` appends a chunk for **every** element with no type filter (and no emptiness check on its short branch), so the builder must filter `element_type == 'figure'` **before** chunking. (b) **`structure-v2` path — the filter must be on the chunker's INPUT, not on its output (V2R-1, corrected):** `[V]` `_merge_class` (`documents/chunking.py:220-225`) returns `prose` for **every** type other than `table`/`list`, grouping is keyed on that class (`:368`, `:398`), and the chunk's single type is computed over the **merged group** by `_chunk_element_type` (`:228`). A `figure` element would therefore be **merged into the surrounding prose** and contribute caption text — or an empty string — to somebody else's chunk, and `DocumentChunk.element_type` (`String(32)` nullable, `documents/models.py:80`) is a product of that group, never copied from one element. So the rule is: **no `figure` element may enter the element list handed to `chunk_elements`** (filter in `documents/routes.py`'s `_build_chunk_objects` at the call site, `[V]` the same place that builds the chunk objects), with the merge class rule as defence in depth; for text elements behaviour is unchanged. Consequence of the rule: a caption-bearing figure produces **exactly one** ordinary chunk (the paragraph's), and a caption-less figure (`content = ''`) produces **no** chunk at all. No section may claim the caption is chunked once unless this exclusion is implemented | BACKEND_AGENT |
| 7. Retrieval path 2 — element-type-aware structural | In `chat/rag.py:988-1071`: (a) the empty-content filter becomes **element-type aware**, retaining `figure` elements; (b) figure seeding is added for explicit figure intent (question terms such as figure/diagram/chart/illustration, combined with the existing section-name or page scope). Figure candidates keep `score = 0.0` and carry only their `structural_score`/`retrieval_path` | AI_AGENT |
| 8. Ranking honesty | Caption-bearing figures rank through the **existing** RRF fusion like any chunk; element-only figures rank by structural score. **No fabricated similarity is ever reported** for a figure | AI_AGENT |
| 9. Citation/provenance | A figure source carries `documentId`, `page`, `elementId`, `caption`, `evidenceKind='figure'` and the retrieval path — plus **`figureRef` only under gate 26 (a)/(b)**; under the no-DDL fallback no `figureRef` is emitted at all | AI_AGENT |
| 10. Frontend | Renders the caption and page always; renders the **image** only through the authorised asset route when gate 26 enables it, otherwise an explicit "image not available in this configuration" state; never raw HTML; never a substituted image | FRONTEND_AGENT |

**Failure handling (all `[P]`, each tested):**

| Failure | Required behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| Figure detected, asset extraction/write failed | **No figure row is persisted** (persist-last ordering, §10.5). The failure is **reported**, not stored: a counter plus the document id and image sequence index are logged and summarised in the ingestion result — `[V]` no column exists to hold a failure reason (`document_structure_elements`, `graph/models.py:162-205`) and the R0 claim that one was persisted is withdrawn. The image is simply not citable; its caption still exists as a paragraph (step 3's authority rule), so no retrieval content is lost. Durable per-figure provenance is an owner decision (gate 25) |
| Figure detected, asset missing at request time | Resolved **from file absence**, not from a persisted flag: the asset route (which exists **only** under gate 26) returns 404 for the owner too, the evidence reports the figure as unavailable, and no placeholder image is ever substituted. Under the no-DDL fallback this row is unreachable because no asset route exists. **K-02 note:** an asset can go missing *because another run's sweep deleted it* — that is precisely the failure K-02 forbids, and it is why this row must not be treated as an acceptable outcome for a concurrency defect |
| Figure has no caption | Content is `''`; only path 2 can surface it (explicit figure intent + structural scope); it is **never** described by invented caption text |
| Foreign document / cross-tenant asset request | Ownership resolved server-side from the JWT; 404 (not 403) for a foreign document, matching existing document routes |
| Caption association ambiguous | No association (`caption = None`); ambiguity is not resolved by guessing |
| A committed element's asset is missing, unreadable or corrupt | Reported as **unavailable** for that figure; **no** placeholder, no substituted image, no fallback to another run's file. Under the no-DDL fallback (gate 26 option c) this is the normal state for every figure and the UI says so explicitly |
| **Two processing runs target the same document (R2-02; CORRECTED in R3 — K-01/K-02)** | **`[V]` The existing status guard does NOT make this impossible.** It is a read-then-act check on a snapshot (`:672-681`) with no lock, no conditional update and no constraint, and the new status is committed only at `:713` — so two requests can both pass it (§2.5 C-1/C-2). Run-scoped names then prevent one *corruption* mode (**no run overwrites a name a committed element references** — G2 still holds), but they do **not** make the outcome safe: *a sweep can still delete the other run's candidate files*, because those files are unreferenced until that run commits (K-02, §2.5 N-005). **Required behaviour therefore:** (a) **Gate 27** must supply a per-document claim so that the second run is refused at acquisition, not merely "usually refused" by the guard; (b) until then, **no claim of concurrency safety may be made anywhere in this plan**, and cleanup must apply an active-run exclusion or delete nothing; (c) the guard stays as a cheap early rejection, and (d) the duplicate candidate files are reported residue **only** if the exclusion mechanism was in force — otherwise the second run's commit may be left referencing files another sweep removed. This row is the plan's single statement of the two-run case; §10.5, §10.6 and §15.1 rows 40-47 defer to it |

### 10.2 `figure` is EXTEND, not REUSE (REV-007)

`[V]` emitting a figure requires **five** vocabulary/parser edits across the extractor and the parser, and the parser fails closed on anything unmapped (`parser.py:186,297-304`) — **plus a sixth requirement (item 6 below) without which the figure would also be chunked and embedded as duplicate text, and a seventh design item (item 7) that is gated**:

1. `documents/processing.py:39` — add the extractor-side type to `ALLOWED_ELEMENT_TYPES` (otherwise `ExtractedElement.__post_init__` raises at `:79-82`).
2. `documents/parser.py:188-193` — add the mapping in `ELEMENT_TYPE_MAP` (today: heading, paragraph, table, text, list — **no** figure).
3. `documents/parser.py:176-182` — add it to `EMITTED_ELEMENT_TYPES` (today: section, paragraph, table, text_block).
4. `documents/processing.py:47-92` — add bbox fields to `ExtractedElement` and populate them for figures. `[V]` **the extractor carries no bbox fields today** (content, element_type, page, heading_level, heading_path, section, table_data, list_items) and uses boxes only locally (`:201-215` table boxes, `:253-258` block boxes) — so a figure bbox needs a genuinely new data path, not just an emit.
5. `documents/parser.py` `_build_elements` (`:~385-421`) — forward that bbox into `ParsedElement.bbox_x0..y1`, which is declared (`:226-229`) and serialised (`:253-256`) but **never populated**, which is why `documents/structure.py:112-140` persists `None` for every element today.
6. **Exclude `figure` from the chunk/embed path (R2-01 — HIGH; this is a *requirement*, not an optional refinement).** `[V]` the ordinary chunk list is built from the extractor's element list (`documents/routes.py:198` → `:125`) and `chunk_text` (`documents/processing.py`) appends one chunk per element with no type filter, so adding `figure` to `ALLOWED_ELEMENT_TYPES` alone would emit **a second chunk containing the caption** and, for a caption-less figure, an **empty** chunk that still gets embedded and counted. The five edits above are therefore incomplete on their own: a filter must be applied to the **element list at the point of consumption**: (a) the list passed to `chunk_text` (legacy) and (b) the list passed to `chunk_elements` (structure-v2) both exclude `figure`, filtered at the call site in `documents/routes.py`'s `_build_chunk_objects` **before** either call — because the structure-v2 chunker merges non-table/non-list types as prose (`[V]` `documents/chunking.py:220-225`, `:368`) and computes the chunk type over the merged group (`:228`), so filtering the resulting object would be too late (V2R-1). §10.1 step 6b is the normative statement; §15.1 rows 35-37 are its tests.
7. **Persist the asset identity — gated (R2-02/R2-03, gate 26; concurrency is gate 27).** The run-scoped asset name cannot be re-derived from `(document_id, sequence_index)` once two runs exist for the same document, so a committed figure can be cited with its image only when its asset identifier is stored (§10.5, gate 26: a nullable `asset_ref` column, a small asset table, or **no DDL** with no asset citation). This item is **not** authorized by this document. **R3 boundary (K-01/K-02):** gate **26 answers *which file* a committed row names**; it does **not** answer *whether two runs may exist at once* or *which files a sweep may delete*. Those are **gate 27** (§10.6) and are **not** absorbed by this item — a reader who treats item 7 as "the asset question" will miss the concurrency requirement entirely.

The public type constant (`parser.py:156`, `graph/models.py:117`) and the graph vocabulary (`graph/models.py:110-131`) are genuinely reused — that part of the original classification was right. **No production consumer of `figure` exists** `[V]` (it has never been emitted), so this change cannot regress current behaviour; the research layer does reference the type and forbids premature use (`backend/evaluation/schemas.py:17`, `backend/evaluation/datasets/structure_discriminating_v1.py:91`), which is the same honesty rule §19 applies.

### 10.3 Equations — explicitly deferred (REV-006)

**Decision in this revision:** `[F]` **equations are not supported and nothing about them is claimed.** Concretely:

* No `backend/documents/equations.py` module (the original plan's C-4 is **withdrawn**).
* No equation element type is introduced; no LaTeX/MathML representation is proposed.
* Equation-like text continues to arrive as ordinary `text_block`/paragraph content, which is **text preservation, not equation understanding** — and this wording must appear wherever the capability is described.
* A test is required that asserts only *preservation*: equation-region text either appears verbatim in a text element or the page is counted as **unaccounted** and the document verdict follows §7.2.1 (`partial` if another page is usable, otherwise `error`); **no test may assert notation correctness**.
* If the owner wants notation support later, it needs its own decision record (engine/dependency, accuracy measurement, storage) before any implementation — gate 18.

### 10.4 Contract A/B/C/E deltas in this revision

| Contract | Original | This revision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A** canonical representation | `figure` "reuse"; equations implied | `figure` = **EXTEND** with the five vocabulary/parser edits plus the chunk-path exclusion and the gated asset-identity item (§10.2 items 1-7); `extraction_method`/`extraction_confidence` only claimable under option A or document-level fields; **equation row removed**; caption stored in the existing `content` column |
| **B** OCR result | per-page record with no home | record exactly as option A's table (§8.3) **if approved**; otherwise document-level honesty only (§8.2) |
| **C** evidence | adds `figure`, `extractionMethod`, `figureRef`, `caption` | same fields, now with the retrieval mechanism (§10.1), the ranking-honesty rule, `elementId`, and the failure matrix |
| **D** answer request/response | adds `marks`, `includeDiagram` | `marks` retained with an honest unsupported state (§11.3); **`includeDiagram` removed from this revision's contract** and deferred to 5b (REV-011); `topK` gains declared bounds (§12.1); **per-section evidence linkage is a required response field for generated answers** (`sectionEvidence`, §12.1) — the review's §12 requirement, now in the contract rather than only in the metrics |
| **E** diagram representation | full payload | unchanged in shape, but **only reachable after gate 9/10** and now explicitly `retrieved_figure`-only until generation is approved |
| **F** processing status | five statuses, definition change | full lifecycle contract with transitions, consumers, failure/retry, tests (§7); the verdict is now a single function (§7.2.1) that every other section defers to |

### 10.5 Figure-asset lifecycle and recovery (C3, C4, R2-02, R2-03; **corrected in R3 by K-01/K-02/K-03**) — **OWNER DECISION: OPTION B**

**R3 reading note.** The twelve-state table, the cleanup procedure, the guarantees and the limitations below were **rewritten in R3** where R2 asserted concurrency safety the code cannot deliver. The concurrency requirement itself is now stated **once**, in **§10.6**, and this section defers to it. The Option-B ordering remains the owner's binding decision; what changed is that the ordering is **necessary and not sufficient**.

**Owner decision (2026-10-14, binding on this design).** Reprocessing is **write first → commit the new structure → clean up the old assets.** The previously committed structure must remain usable if a replacement run fails before its commit. *Documentation only:* this authorizes no implementation, no schema change, no migration, no production edit. This section is the **single normative statement** of the asset lifecycle; every other section refers to it.

**Non-atomicity stated up front.** `[V]` filesystem writes and database writes are **not** one transaction in this project, and the codebase already documents the consequence: `delete_document` commits the database **first** and then removes the file **best-effort** ("If file deletion fails, the DB is already clean — the orphaned file can be cleaned up later", `documents/routes.py:612-634`), while `cleanup_new_file` (`storage/file_store.py:201-214`) is a defensive rollback helper, not a transaction. This design does not pretend otherwise.

**Two verified constraints shape the design.**

* `[V]` **`delete_upload` deletes regular files only.** It refuses anything else explicitly (`[V]` `storage/file_store.py:188` `lstat`, `:191-192` `stat.S_ISREG` refusal, `:189-190` except/return) and refuses symlinks/reparse points and paths outside the root (`:170-187`). A recursive directory delete **does not exist** in the audited primitives and must not be improvised.
* `[V]` asset *listing* and asset *writing* also do not exist; only `save_upload` `:31`, `delete_upload` `:137`, `cleanup_new_file` `:201` and the containment helpers `_is_link_or_reparse_point` `:80`, `_resolved_within` `:97`.

**Asset identity is run-scoped and immutable (`[P]`, R2-02).** Assets are individual **regular files** under the per-user upload subtree (`[V]` `_user_upload_dir` `storage/file_store.py:24`), named

```
<document_id>__<run_id>__<figure_sequence_index>.<ext>
```

* `run_id` is a **server-generated identifier for one processing run** (never supplied or influenced by a client). `<ext>` is fixed by the server (write contract below).
* **Candidate set** = the files a given run wrote for a document. **Committed set** = the files named by the elements of that document's currently committed structure. The two name spaces are **disjoint for different runs**, so a run can never create, truncate or rename over a name the committed structure references.
* **R1's deterministic `<document_id>__<figure_sequence_index>.<ext>` naming is withdrawn.** With one name per slot, a second run necessarily overwrote the bytes a committed row still pointed at, and no ordering of writes and commits could prevent it — that was the real defect behind R1's "bytes overwritten" limitation. Run-scoped names remove the condition instead of documenting it.
* Residue therefore changes character: it is always a file **no** committed element references (a superseded run's file, or a candidate from a run that never committed).

**Resolution needs one persisted identifier — gate 26 `[D]`.** A reader maps a committed figure element to *its* run's file. Because several files can exist for the same `(document_id, figure_sequence_index)`, a derived `figureRef` cannot identify the right one; the identifier must be stored:

| Option | What it adds | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **(a) nullable `asset_ref` on `document_structure_elements`** (preferred) | one additive column holding the run-scoped file name | `figureRef = "fig:<asset_ref>"`, resolved server-side with an ownership check; one additive migration `008_*`, no existing column touched, `DATABASE_AGENT`-authored |
| **(b) a `document_figure_assets` table** | element ↔ asset ↔ run mapping | larger surface; useful only if per-asset metadata (bytes, format, checksum) is also wanted |
| **(c) no DDL** | nothing | **the fallback:** figures are still extracted, persisted and retrievable by caption/structure, and run-scoped assets are still written — but **no `figureRef` is emitted and no asset route is enabled**, because the resolver cannot prove which run's file belongs to a committed row. The UI shows the caption and an explicit "image not available in this configuration" state |

The plan does **not** pretend documentation alone closes this gap: with today's schema (`[V]` `graph/models.py:162-205` has no such column) the guarantee is complete for **writes** and incomplete for **resolution**. That is exactly what gate 26 decides. **`[V]` The guarantee is *also* incomplete for concurrency, and that is a different gap with a different gate:** gate 26 answers *which file* a committed row names; it does **not** prevent two runs from existing, and it does **not** tell a sweep which files belong to an active run. **Gate 27 (§10.6) is the concurrency decision, it is not approved, and Phase 2a is blocked on it independently of gate 26.** R2's text here implied the asset question was fully covered by gate 26; it was not (K-01/K-02).

**Write contract (`[P]`, R2-03).** The write side is specified here for the first time; R1 specified only read/serve, listing and deletion.

| Concern | Required rule |
| --- | --- |
| Format and extension | **Fixed server-side to one allowed image format** (e.g. PNG). Never derived from document content, embedded filename, declared MIME or client input. The asset route sets an explicit image `Content-Type` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` |
| Path derivation | Destination = the document owner's upload subtree + the run-scoped basename, built through the storage layer's containment helpers only. No ad-hoc path joining anywhere in the codebase |
| Containment | The resolved destination must lie inside the document owner's subtree; the write refuses `..`, absolute escapes, symlink/reparse-point ancestors and any destination outside the root — the same discipline the deletion primitive already applies (`[V]` `:170-199`) |
| Destination shape | The writer refuses to write when the destination exists as anything other than a regular file (including a symlink or a directory) |
| Collision | Creation is **exclusive** and fails closed if the final name already exists: with run-scoped names a collision is a bug, never an expected replacement |
| Completeness | Bytes go to a temporary name in the same directory; the final name appears **only** through an atomic rename, so a partially written file never carries a final name. Abandoned temp files are residue, handled by the same reconciliation |
| Run association | The asset belongs to the run that wrote it (its name carries `run_id`) and, once gate 26 approves (a) or (b), to the committed element through `asset_ref`. No asset is ever associated by guesswork or by timestamp |
| Never replace a referenced asset | Defensive invariant: the writer must not create, truncate or rename over a name referenced by the currently committed structure. (With run-scoped names this cannot arise; the rule exists so a future change cannot reintroduce it) |
| Primitive reuse | **Reused:** containment helpers, `delete_upload` (per-file deletion), the defensive-cleanup pattern of `cleanup_new_file`. **New helpers (`BACKEND_AGENT`, `storage/file_store.py`):** run-scoped write, containment-checked read/serve (§10.1 step 5b) and containment-checked listing. `[V]` `save_upload:31` forces a UUID name and **cannot** be used for run-scoped figure writes |

**Lifecycle: the twelve required states `[P]`.**

| # | State | Required behaviour |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | A new processing run begins | The previous committed structure **and** its assets are untouched: the run writes only its own run-scoped names. **Nothing is deleted at the start** — this is the change from today's up-front delete (`[V]` `:696-697`, `:701-705`, which remain current behaviour only, R2-02). **`[V]` today's delete runs inside the same transaction as the replacement (`:696-713`), so the required ordering cannot be reached by reordering statements — the delete must move to the post-commit sweep, which is a transaction-shape change governed by Gate 27 (§2.5 N-004, K-01)** |
| 2 | New asset writes succeed | They are **candidates**: unreferenced until the run's commit |
| 3 | One or more new asset writes fail | No figure row is written for those figures (persist-last); each failure is reported (counter + document id + sequence index). The run may still commit the rest; page-level effects on the verdict follow §7.2.1 only |
| 4 | New structure persistence fails | The **previous committed structure remains authoritative and citable**, with its images intact. Candidate files are unreferenced residue, prunable per file. Status is `error` for the attempt — never a half-replaced structure |
| 5 | **Crash** after some writes, before commit (**corrected in R3 — K-03/A-3**) | Same as state 4, **but with the status behaviour stated correctly:** the crash rolls the whole transaction back (§2.5 C-4), so the document retains its **previous committed status** (typically `ready`), which is the honest description of the state it is actually in — the previous committed structure and its assets are intact and citable, and the status still describes them. **A stale `processing` row is NOT left behind, and the existing stale-run path is NOT what recovers this case** (`[V]` `:321-330`/`:415-416` can only act on a *committed* `processing` row, which today's code never writes — §2.5 C-3/C-5). For a crash during an **initial upload**, there is no document row at all after the rollback; the stored file is an orphan upload, handled by the existing orphan-upload class (`[V]` `backend/scripts/cleanup_orphan_uploads.py`, dry-run by default, aborts if the database cannot be read). The candidate assets the crashed run wrote are unreferenced residue. **If Gate 27 introduces a committed claim, this state changes**: a claim can then be left behind, and stale-claim recovery becomes a real requirement with its own semantics (Gate 27, §10.6) |
| 6 | Commit succeeds, cleanup fails | The new structure is authoritative and self-consistent; the previous run's files remain as reported residue, no committed element references them, and they are prunable per file by the approved cleanup **subject to the active-run exclusion (§10.6, G3″): "prunable" means eligible for a sweep that can prove both non-reference *and* non-activity — not that any sweep at any time may delete them.** **(Qualifier added after review: this row was unconditional in the first R3 draft and could be quoted back into the A-2 assumption.)** |
| 7 | Cleanup interrupted partway | The unswept files are **identifiable and reportable**, and are still non-referenced at the time of the interruption; the sweep is **resumable and idempotent** in the sense that re-running it neither double-deletes nor touches a referenced or active file — **but only once the active-run exclusion exists (§10.6).** Without it, "still unreferenced" is not a safe description of those files (another run may hold them), so while Gate 27 is undecided this state is **report-only**. **(Qualifier added after review.)** |
| 8 | Retry after a failed or interrupted run | A **new run id**; previous candidate residue is swept only when provably unreferenced; no double-write of rows or assets |
| 9 | Document deleted | The existing ordering is kept (`[V]` DB commit first, then best-effort file removal, `:612-634`); every asset the document owns is then deleted **per file** with `delete_upload`. `[V]` today's path removes only `doc.stored_path`: **asset deletion is a new code path, not a new mechanism** |
| 10 | Old or abandoned asset with no live reference | A reconciliation is **read-only first** (containment-checked listing + classification into §10.5's six classes); deletion is a **separate, approval-gated** step, per file, never a directory walk, and **only for files whose run state is determinable and inactive (§10.6, G3″)**. "No live reference" is **not** by itself the deletion test — the six-class table is. **(Qualifier added after review.)** |
| 11 | A committed reference points at a missing or corrupt asset | Reported **unavailable** for that figure; nothing substituted, guessed or served from another run. Under gate 26 option (c) this is the normal state for every figure, and it is stated in the UI |
| 12 | **Concurrent or repeated attempts on one document (REWRITTEN in R3 — K-01/K-02; the R2 text was wrong)** | **`[V]` The status guard does not prevent this.** It is a read-then-act check on a snapshot with no lock, conditional update or constraint, and the new status commits only at the end of the request — so two requests can both pass it (§2.5 C-1/C-2, and row "Two processing runs" in §10.1). With two runs live: **run-scoped names still prevent one run from overwriting a name a committed element references (G2)**, but **cleanup is NOT safe** — a sweep that asks "is this file referenced by the committed structure?" matches the *other* run's not-yet-committed candidates and would delete them, after which that run commits a structure referencing deleted files (§2.5 N-005, K-02). **Required behaviour (Gate 27):** a per-document claim that excludes a second run **and** an active-run exclusion in cleanup. **Until both exist, this plan claims no concurrency safety for Phase 2a**, cleanup must delete nothing it cannot prove unreferenced **and** provably inactive (§10.5 cleanup procedure, step vi), and no acceptance criterion may assume the second run was refused. **Repeated *sequential* attempts are safe**: a retry is a new run with a new identifier |

**Concurrency, retry and idempotency (REWRITTEN in R3 — K-01/K-02/K-03).** R2 wrote: *"The only synchronization assumed is the one that exists (the status guard) … no new lock, transaction boundary or distributed coordination is assumed"* and concluded that even a bypassed guard produced "churn, not corruption". **Both halves of that were wrong and are withdrawn.**

* **The existing guard is not synchronization.** `[V]` it is a snapshot read plus a Python predicate, with no lock, no conditional update and no constraint, and the new status commits only at `:713` (§2.5 C-1/C-2). Two runs can overlap.
* **A retry is safe; two runs are not.** A **sequential** retry is a new run with a new identifier, so retries are safe by construction and the acceptance criterion is *identical derived rows and exactly one live asset set* (§15.1 rows 23, 34) — **"with no orphan growth" has been removed** (K-04/A-4) because cleanup is best-effort and reconciliation is approval-gated; the measurable replacement criteria are §15.5. **Concurrent** runs are a different property and are **not** safe (§10.1's two-run row, §2.5 N-005).
* **No mechanism is assumed, because none exists.** No lock, claim, lease, run registry or conditional update is present in the code (§2.5 C-2), and this plan **selects none**. The required properties, the eleven cases that must be handled and the owner decision are **Gate 27** (§10.6). Until Gate 27 is decided, the only defensible position is the one this section now takes: **Phase 2a's asset lifecycle is not concurrency-safe, and the plan says so.**

**Asset classes cleanup must distinguish (K-02/A-2).** Before any cleanup design can be called safe, it must classify a file into exactly one of six classes, and **only one of them is deletable**:

| Class | Definition | Deletable? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Referenced by the committed structure** | the file name equals the `asset_ref` (or equivalent identifier) of an element in the document's **currently committed** structure | **No** — never, under any ordering |
| **Belonging to an active run** | the file's run identifier matches a run that holds, or is eligible to hold, the document's claim (a run that has started and has not committed, failed or been superseded) | **No** — this is the class R2's predicate failed to exclude (N-005) |
| **From a failed run** | a run that raised and whose transaction rolled back | **Only when provably unreferenced *and* provably not active** — otherwise report |
| **From an interrupted run** | a run whose process died (no row, no status change — §2.5 C-4) | **Only under the same two proofs** — and its "not active" status cannot be proven by a timestamp alone (§10.6) |
| **From an older successfully replaced run** | the run that wrote the currently committed structure has superseded it | **Yes**, when unreferenced and inactive — the ordinary sweep case |
| **Unknown / undeterminable** | run state cannot be read (database unavailable, row unreadable, listing uncertain, run identifier absent) | **No — delete nothing and report.** This is the **fail-safe** class |

**Cleanup procedure (approval-gated, per file) — REWRITTEN in R3.**

* (i) Classify read-only, and **classify before deleting anything** — the sweep never interleaves classification with deletion.
* (ii) Determine the **active-run set** for the document (the mechanism is Gate 27; if no mechanism exists, the active-run set is *unknown* and step (vi) applies for every candidate).
* (iii) **Exclude every file whose run is in the active set, and every file whose run state is unknown.**
* (iv) For each remaining candidate, re-read the **currently committed** reference set and delete only regular files inside the document owner's subtree that are **referenced by nothing** — accepting that this re-read is **necessary but not sufficient** (R2 assumed it was sufficient; it is not — a concurrent run's write can land between the re-read and the unlink).
* (v) Never walk a directory tree, never delete a directory, never delete another document's or another user's file.
* (vi) If **any** of the following cannot be established — non-reference, non-activity, containment, regular-file shape, or run identity — **delete nothing and report**. "Unsure" always resolves to *keep*.
* (vii) The safe default while Gate 27 is undecided is **report-only**: the classification and listing run, deletions do not.

`[V]` The fail-safe shape is not novel here: the project's existing orphan-upload tooling (`backend/scripts/cleanup_orphan_uploads.py`) is **dry-run by default**, **aborts rather than deleting when the database cannot be read** ("It never treats an unreachable database as 'everything is an orphan'"), and archives-and-verifies before deleting — the same discipline this procedure requires. That script's reference set is `documents.stored_path`/`verification_results.stored_path` and is therefore **orthogonal** to figure assets; asset cleanup is a new consumer of the same *pattern*, not a new mechanism.

**Every cleanup claim in this plan depends on all five of these being true (R4-06, completed in R4).** A statement about cleanup is only as strong as the weakest condition below. States 5, 6, 7, 10 and 12, the guarantees table and §16.2 all defer to this list; none of them may be quoted in isolation as an unconditional safety statement.

| # | Condition a cleanup claim depends on | Status today |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **CP-1** | **Sequential or otherwise properly coordinated execution** — one run per document, or an equivalent coordination mechanism | **NOT ESTABLISHED.** The status guard is not an atomic claim (§2.5 C-1/C-2); Gate 27 is undecided |
| **CP-2** | **Exclusion of active runs** — the sweep can identify the files of a run that is still running or still able to commit, and skips them | **NOT ESTABLISHED.** Requires a run registry/claim that does not exist (G3″, §10.6 property 8) |
| **CP-3** | **Exclusion of indeterminate runs** — when run state cannot be established, the files are treated as undeletable | **Specified but only exercisable once CP-2 exists**: the fail-safe rule is stated (§10.5 procedure (vi), §15.1 row 47), and it is only meaningful when "active" is a decidable question |
| **CP-4** | **Verified containment and file identity** — the destination resolves inside the document owner's subtree, is a regular file, is not a symlink/reparse point, and its name matches the run-scoped pattern | **Available today**: the audited primitives already refuse non-regular files and out-of-root paths (`[V]` `storage/file_store.py:170-199`), and the write contract (§10.5) requires the same discipline |
| **CP-5** | **No deletion when ownership or run state cannot be established** — uncertainty resolves to *keep and report*, never to delete | **Specified**: procedure (vi) and §15.4's phrase table; the precedent is the existing dry-run/abort orphan tool |

**Cleanup is NOT implemented, and this section is not evidence that it is (R4-06).** `[V]` the repository contains **no** figure-asset write helper, **no** containment-checked asset listing, **no** run registry and **no** asset sweep (`storage/file_store.py` exposes only `save_upload`, `delete_upload`, `cleanup_new_file` and the containment helpers — §10.5's "two verified constraints"). Everything specified above is **required behaviour for future work**, gated at Gate 27 and Phase 2a. Specifying how cleanup must behave, and under what conditions its claims hold, is **not** a claim that any deletion path exists today, and no phase report may cite this section as implemented behaviour.

**Guarantees this design does provide `[P]` — and one it does NOT (revised in R3).**

Each guarantee now carries the **condition** under which it holds, because R2 stated G1-G8 unconditionally and three of them are conditional in fact. Verdict vocabulary: **HOLDS (sequential)** = true for one run at a time, the case the current code actually produces; **NOT ESTABLISHED (concurrent)** = requires Gate 27.

| # | Guarantee | Holds (sequential) | Not established (concurrent) — needs Gate 27 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **G1** | A failed replacement never destroys the previous committed structure or its images: nothing is deleted before the replacement is committed | **yes** — the transaction rolls back and the up-front delete is undone with it (§2.5 C-7/C-4) | — |
| **G2** | A committed structure never references bytes another run **overwrote**: names are run-scoped and never rewritten; resolution derives from the row's own `asset_ref` (gate 26) or the asset is not cited at all | **yes** | **yes** — run-scoped names hold under concurrency too (this is the one guarantee concurrency does not weaken). **Condition made explicit after review:** this depends on the run identifier being **collision-free**; §10.6 property 3 now requires that, and the guarantee is void if two runs could generate the same identifier |
| **G3′** | Cleanup never deletes an asset **the committed structure references**, and deletes nothing when it cannot prove non-reference | **yes** | **NOT ESTABLISHED.** The old G3 said "an asset the committed structure **may** reference" and that is the wrong test: a concurrent run's candidate is *not yet* referenced, so the predicate does not protect it (§2.5 N-005). Reference-only is **necessary, not sufficient** |
| **G3″** | Cleanup never deletes an asset belonging to an **active run**, and deletes nothing when run state is unknown | **yes** (trivially — there is only one run) | **NOT ESTABLISHED — this is the K-02/A-2 defect.** Requires a run registry/claim and an active-run exclusion; **no such mechanism exists or is designed** |
| **G4** | A missing, unreadable or corrupt asset is reported unavailable; nothing is substituted, guessed or served from another run's file | **yes** | **yes** — but note that under G3″'s failure mode an asset can go missing *because a sweep removed it*, and G4 reporting it as "unavailable" would then be honest-but-downstream of a defect |
| **G5** | Cross-document and cross-tenant resolution is impossible (JWT-scoped document row → that user's subtree → containment + symlink/reparse refusal; 404 for a foreign document) | **yes** | **yes** |
| **G6** | No destructive operation is invented: every deletion is one regular file through the audited primitive; nothing walks or removes a directory tree | **yes** | **yes** |
| **G7** | No filesystem/database atomicity is claimed anywhere, and none is assumed | **yes** | **yes** (K-07/B-6 restates this for the DB-row claims too) |
| **G8** | Every residue state is (a) unreferenced and (b) reported, never silent | **yes** | **NOT ESTABLISHED as stated.** A concurrent run's candidate is "residue" only from the other run's point of view; it is *live working state*. "Unreferenced ⇒ residue" is exactly the inference K-02 forbids |
| **G9** | **At most one run processes a document at a time, and a run that has lost its claim cannot commit** | **yes** (trivially) | **NOT ESTABLISHED — REQUIRED (Gate 27)** |
| **G10** | **A crashed run's claim is recoverable without assuming that a timed-out worker has stopped** | n/a (no claim today) | **NOT ESTABLISHED — REQUIRED (Gate 27)** |

**Note added after review:** **G7** has no acceptance test and deliberately so — it is a statement about *claims*, not about runtime state, and it is enforced by §15.4's wording table rather than by a test. A reader should not look for a G7 test row; the reader should look for violations of §15.4.

**Reading rule.** G1, G2, G4-G7 are properties of the **single-run** case, which is the case today's code actually produces. **G3′, G3″, G8, G9 and G10 are the properties the Option-B asset lifecycle *needs*, and every one of them depends on Gate 27 being decided and implemented.** No section may cite G1-G8 as evidence that Phase 2a is safe to build under concurrency, and no acceptance criterion may assume G9 or G10.

**Limitations this design does NOT remove `[D]` (rewritten in R3).**

* **`[D]` The asset lifecycle is not concurrency-safe without Gate 27 — this is now the plan's headline limitation, not a footnote.** R2's replacement for this bullet was: *"If the status guard were bypassed, two runs could both commit; the last commit wins and the losing run's assets become unreferenced residue. That is churn, not corruption of the committed set."* **That sentence is withdrawn as incorrect (K-02/A-2).** It assumed the harm is confined to the loser's own residue; in fact the *winner's* sweep can delete the *loser's* candidate files, and the loser then commits a structure referencing assets that no longer exist. The failure is therefore **not** self-contained churn. It is gated at Gate 27 and summarised in §10.6. **Phase 2a must not be started as if this were safe.**
* **Gate 26 unresolved (option c) ⇒ no image is ever cited**, and the assets written are residue by design until the decision. Rationale: a resolver that cannot prove which run a file belongs to must not serve it.
* **Cleanup is best-effort and bounded, not complete.** A crash between the commit and the sweep leaves unreferenced files until the next run or an approved reconciliation; they are **reported, bounded per document and identified**, and they contain no referenced bytes. **Zero-orphan growth is not promised** (K-04/A-4) — §15.5 gives the criteria that are actually checkable.
* **Recovery after an interruption is not automatic.** `[V]` today a crash leaves no claim and no `processing` row; the previous committed state survives and the residue is reported (§2.5 C-4). A future claim mechanism **adds** a stale-claim recovery requirement that does not exist today (G10, Gate 27).
* **`processing_started_at is None` is treated as stale** (`[V]` `:325-326`), so any future claim **must** be committed together with its timestamp in one statement; a timestamp-less claim would be superseded immediately. This is a **required property** of whatever Gate 27 selects, not an implementation detail.
* **Genuine cross-store atomicity is still not claimed** and would require a new mechanism (bytes in the database, or a transactional filesystem). The asset-set half of R1's gate 25 is resolved by this design plus gate 26; **gate 25 remains the home for durable per-figure provenance** (asset version, per-figure failure reason).

### 10.6 Per-document run claim and active-run exclusion — REQUIRED, NOT DELIVERED (Gate 27) `[D]`

**Status of this section.** It is a **requirement statement and an owner decision**, not a design and not an authorization. Nothing in it has been implemented, no mechanism has been selected, and **Gate 27 is not approved**. It exists because R2's Option-B guarantee cannot be delivered without it and R2 implied otherwise.

**The requirement, stated as required properties (not as a preferred mechanism).** The Option-B asset lifecycle needs **all** of the following to be true. They are the properties the owner is being asked to decide *how* to obtain:

1. **Exclusive claim.** At most one run may hold a document at a time, and the hold must be acquired **atomically** — one statement or one short transaction, not a read followed by a write.
2. **Committed before work.** The claim must be **visible to other transactions before the expensive work begins**; a claim that is only visible at the end of the request is not a claim (that is exactly today's guard, §2.5 C-1/C-3).
3. **Run identity, collision-resistant, with a documented source of truth.** The claim must carry a **server-generated run identifier** that is persisted, so a later commit can prove it belongs to the claiming run and a sweep can tell an active run's files from a superseded run's files. **R4-08 (completed in R4):** the identifier **must not collide**, or guarantee **G2** (run-scoped names are never a shared name space) is void — a collision would put two runs' files in one name space precisely where the design claims disjointness, and could cause one run to overwrite or delete another's assets. The full requirement set is the table below; it states *what must be true*, not *how to achieve it*.
4. **Claim acquisition and release.** Acquisition, voluntary release on success, and release on failure must each be defined, including what happens to the claim when the request ends **without either** — a **request timeout**, a **client disconnect** or an **abnormal termination** (`R5-A2`). **(R5: the observable consequence of each is now a criterion — AC-9…AC-12 for the document/asset state — while the claim-state consequence (released, held until expiry, or reclaimable, and within what bound) remains an open requirement marked `DEFERRED` in §15.8. Naming it is not satisfying it.)**
5. **Stale-claim detection and recovery.** A claim must be recoverable after a crash. **`[V]` A timeout or a stale timestamp alone does not prove that a worker has stopped** (`_is_stale_processing` is a heuristic over `processing_started_at`, `:321-328`), so recovery must define what evidence is required and what happens to the possibly-still-running worker.
6. **Old-worker resumption.** If the previous worker is in fact still alive after its claim was reclaimed, its behaviour must be defined, not hoped for.
7. **Commit after claim loss.** **A run that has lost its claim must not be permitted to commit** — or, if it is permitted, the owner must accept the consequence and the plan must say what it is. This is the case R2 never considered.
8. **Active-run exclusion in cleanup.** Cleanup must be able to answer "is this file's run active?" and must delete nothing when it cannot (G3″, §10.5's six classes).
9. **Crash and retry semantics.** The claim's behaviour across process death, container restart, request timeout and client disconnect must be stated **for each separately** — they differ in what the runtime does to the process, the request context and the connection, and a single statement about "crashes" does not cover them (`R5-A2`). **(R5: each scenario's document/asset consequence is now a criterion (AC-4, AC-9…AC-13); each scenario's claim-state consequence is `DEFERRED` in §15.8 and remains an unresolved Gate 27 requirement.)**
10. **Required schema/transaction effects.** Whatever is selected, the plan must record whether it needs DDL (a column or a claim/lease table — `DATABASE_AGENT`-authored, additive, `008`/`009` per §13.1's numbering note), a transaction-shape change in `reprocess_document` (moving the delete to the post-commit sweep — §2.5 N-004), or a lock the current architecture does not use. **Any of these is future gated work and none is authorized.**
11. **Tests before Phase 2a acceptance.** The concurrency and failure-injection acceptance criteria of §15.6 and §15.7 must be met **before** Phase 2a is accepted.

**Run-identifier requirements (`R4-08`, completed in R4) — properties the owner's Gate 27 mechanism must satisfy.** These are **requirements**, not a design: no identifier scheme, column, table, algorithm or dependency is selected or authorized here.

| # | Requirement | Why it is required | Where it is checked |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **RI-1** | **Server-generated.** The identifier is produced by the server for one processing run. It is **never supplied, seeded, derived from, or influenced by** client input (filename, content, headers, request body) or by document content | a client-influenced identifier could be chosen to collide with another run's name space, which is the whole thing G2 relies on | §15.4 phrase rule; §16.2 (ingest keyed by server-owned identity, never client-supplied); §15.6 AC-8 |
| **RI-2** | **Collision-resistant for the practical horizon.** The generation scheme must make accidental collision implausible **and** name its uniqueness expectation (for example: globally unique for the deployment's lifetime, or unique per document, with the bound stated) | G2 requires the two runs' name spaces to be disjoint; a scheme whose collision probability is unstated cannot support the claim | Gate 27 (**the expectation must be written down**, since "unique" without a scope is not a testable property) |
| **RI-3** | **Documented source of truth.** Where the identifier lives (claim row, lease table, column, or equivalent) and which component owns it are stated explicitly, with one writer | a sweep that cannot read the identifier cannot exclude an active run (CP-2) | §10.5 class table; §15.7 CC-6/CC-7 |
| **RI-4** | **Lifetime and scope documented.** When the identifier is created, how long it is authoritative, what supersedes it, and what happens to it after the run ends successfully, fails, or is abandoned | recovery and cleanup both need to know whether an identifier is still live; a bare timestamp does not answer that | §10.6 property 5; §15.6 AC-4/AC-7 |
| **RI-5** | **Not exposed in a way that lets a client act on it.** If it ever appears in an API response (it need not), it must not be usable to select, address or mutate another run's assets | the asset route is an IDOR/BOLA target; identity must be resolved from the JWT-scoped document row | §10.5 write contract; §10.5 G5; §15.1 row 20 |
| **RI-6** | **Collision or duplication fails closed: never overwrite, never delete.** If the server ever observes a duplicate or colliding identifier, it must **refuse the write**, **preserve both runs' files**, and **report** the condition. It must **not** resolve the collision by overwriting, by reusing the name, or by deleting the other run's assets | the design's central promise is that no run overwrites or deletes another run's bytes (G2, G3″); a collision is the one case where that promise is at risk, so it must fail safe | §15.6 **AC-8**; §15.1 **row 48**; §15.7 CC-1 |
| **RI-7** | **Adopting an existing convention is a decision, not an assumption.** `[V]` a run-identifier convention already exists **in the research layer** (`backend/evaluation/artifacts.py:77` `def new_run_id(...)`, asserted unique and sortable by its unit-test contract). It is cited as a **precedent only**: production may **not** import from `evaluation/` (`AGENTS.md` §03 forbids the direction), so reusing or mirroring it requires an **ownership and dependency decision** before any production code depends on it | prevents a reader from treating an existing helper as an available implementation | Gate 27; and, if mirroring is chosen, the owning agent's agreement (no ownership contract is edited by this revision) |

**No mechanism is selected by RI-1 … RI-7.** They constrain whatever Gate 27 chooses and add **no** dependency, no schema and no code. In particular, this revision does **not** authorize a lock, a lease, a claim table or a column, and does not authorize importing, copying or depending on the research-layer helper.

**Candidate mechanisms — for owner evaluation only; selecting one is not authorized.** These are listed to make the decision concrete, not to recommend any of them. Each is a *possible* way to obtain properties 1-11; none has been evaluated against this codebase.

| Candidate | Sketch | What it would need here | Open questions |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **(a) Atomic conditional update** | take the claim with a single `UPDATE ... WHERE id = :id AND (status <> 'processing' OR claim_is_reclaimable) RETURNING id`, treating "0 rows updated" as "someone else holds it" | an extra column for run identity; a transaction shape in which the claim commits before the pipeline runs | is a single-statement claim enough for the whole pipeline, or is a lease needed? how is a crashed claim reclaimed? |
| **(b) Short claim transaction (row lock)** | `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` (or `with_for_update`) on the document row for the duration of the claim, committing the run identity before the pipeline | a short dedicated transaction, which the current single-transaction handler does not have | holding a row lock across a multi-minute OCR run is not acceptable; so this collapses into (a) or (c) in practice |
| **(c) Lease / run record** | a run/lease table (or columns) holding `run_id`, owner, `leased_at`, `expires_at`, `state`; acquisition is an atomic insert/conditional update, expiry is explicit | DDL (`DATABASE_AGENT`); defined clock and expiry semantics; a reclaim path | how is a still-running worker stopped before its claim is reclaimed? what if the clock is wrong? |
| **(d) Another mechanism** | anything that provably yields properties 1-11 in *this* architecture | must be argued from the code, not assumed | — |

**Explicitly rejected as sufficient on their own (each was considered and does not deliver the property).**

* **The existing status guard** — a snapshot read; not atomic (§2.5 C-1).
* **Run-scoped asset filenames** — they prevent *overwriting* (G2) but not *deleting an active run's candidates* (§2.5 N-005).
* **Re-reading the committed reference set immediately before each deletion** — necessary but not sufficient; a concurrent write can land between the re-read and the unlink, and the read cannot see an uncommitted run's files as "active".
* **A timeout / stale timestamp** — cannot distinguish a dead worker from a slow one (§2.5 C-5).
* **"Last commit wins"** — R2's position; it is rejected because the loser's commit can reference assets the winner's sweep deleted (K-02).

**Eleven cases this requirement must cover** (each becomes an acceptance criterion, not an implementation claim): (1) concurrent reprocess requests; (2) concurrent initial uploads of the same content hash; (3) a claim that goes stale; (4) a worker that exceeds the current 600-second staleness threshold while still running; (5) a worker crash followed by a retry; (6) lease expiry while the older worker is still running; (7) an older run attempting to commit after a newer run took the claim; (8) a failed database commit; (9) a failed asset write; (10) recovery after an interruption; (11) cleanup encountering a document with no determinable active run. The proposed acceptance criteria are §15.6 and §15.7. **R5:** the eight lifecycle-ending and environmental scenarios these eleven cases imply are enumerated **individually**, each with its protected invariant, expected observable behaviour, requirement identifier, criterion or explicit `DEFERRED` marker, and verification class, in **§15.8** — which is the **only** place a scenario's coverage status is assigned.

**Relationship to the other gates — stated so the two are not conflated.**

| Gate | Question it answers | Does it answer concurrency? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **26** | **Which file** does a committed figure element name? (asset identity: an `asset_ref` column, an asset table, or no DDL) | **No.** Gate 26 can be approved and Phase 2a still be concurrency-unsafe |
| **27** | **Who may run**, for how long, under what identity, and **what cleanup may therefore delete?** | **Yes.** This is the gate the Option-B guarantee depends on |
| **5** | Is figure extraction in scope at all? | No |

**Standing statement, repeated for emphasis.** **Gate 27 is not approved. Gate 26 remains an asset-identity decision and must not silently absorb concurrency control. Phase 2a remains blocked until the necessary owner decisions are made and the resulting design is approved. Adding this gate does not authorize implementation, and it authorizes no schema, transaction or lock change.** If the owner's selected mechanism needs DDL, that is future gated work (`DATABASE_AGENT`, additive, per §13.1's numbering note); if it needs a transaction-shape change in `reprocess_document`, that is Phase 2a work under the Gate 27 decision (§2.5 N-004).

## 11. Answer-Generation and Model Approval Gates (REV-012)

### 11.1 Verified starting point

* `[V]` the QA path is a **single-span extractive** model with a 384-token input window and 100-token answer cap; a probe returned a 16-character span for a factual question and score `0.0` for an unsupported one (audit findings 7-9, re-confirmed in the original plan §1.2).
* `[V]` `AskResponse` has no format/marks/diagram fields (`chat/routes.py:116-124`); the frontend mirror matches (`src/lib/api.ts:316-321`).
* `[V]` `.env.example` reserves commented `LLM_API_KEY` / `LLM_MODEL`; nothing reads them.

### 11.2 Decision required, not made

`[D]` Whether a generation model is introduced, and whether it is local (no egress, needs measured capacity) or hosted (egress + cost + a rule change), remains **gate 7/8**. This revision records the alternatives and the evaluation requirements; it does **not** select a model, and `[B]` no latency/memory/capacity measurement exists in this environment, so no option can be called feasible today.

### 11.3 Honest behaviour while generation is denied or unavailable (REV-012)

`[P]` specified for Phase 4 and mandatory in Phase 6:

| Situation | Required response | Required UI |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `marks` absent | today's behaviour exactly: extractive span, `answerFormat="extractive"` | unchanged |
| `marks` present, generation **not approved / not configured** | extractive answer, `answerFormat="extractive"`, `marksRequested` echoed, `marksSupported=false`, `outcome`/`generation.degraded=true` with `reason`; **never padded, never relabelled as an exam answer** | an explicit "marks-based answers are not available" state; no fake long answer, no word-count padding |
| Generation configured but fails / times out | honest failure state (`UNAVAILABLE`/`degraded=true` + reason); never a fabricated or silently truncated answer | error state with retry affordance, distinct from an empty answer |
| Evidence insufficient | existing gate outcome (`[V]` `brain/gate.py`) unchanged; generation adds no new authority to answer | existing insufficient-evidence state |

### 11.4 Evaluation requirements before any generation release (thresholds are gate 21)

`[P]` no release without separately-reported metrics for: answer accuracy against evidence; evidence coverage; groundedness/unsupported-claim rate; structural completeness per mark level (assessed by rubric, **never** by word count alone); redundancy; citation correctness (document + page); latency p50/p95; and failure/abstention rates. `[D]` every numeric threshold is an owner decision (no defensible baseline exists today); the plan invents none.

---

## 12. API and Frontend Compatibility Requirements (REV-011, REV-014)

### 12.1 `AskRequest` / `AskResponse` deltas

`[P]` `chat/routes.py:26-28` gains only what can be honoured:

```
AskRequest
  question : str                  # unchanged, non-empty
  topK     : int = 5              # NOW validated: ge=1, le=<declared ceiling>  [D] ceiling value
  marks    : int | None = None    # one of {6,8,10,15}; None = current behaviour
  # includeDiagram: NOT added in this revision (REV-011) — see §12.3

AskResponse (additive; existing fields untouched)
  answerFormat     : "extractive" | "exam"
  marksRequested   : int | None
  marksSupported   : bool
  diagram          : DiagramPayload | None    # only after gate 9/10
  generation       : { model: str, degraded: bool, reason: str | None }
  sectionEvidence  : dict[str, list[str]] | None   # NEW: per-answer-section evidence ids (generated answers only)
```

* `[V]` `topK` is currently unvalidated (`chat/routes.py:28`, used at `:336`). Bounding it is a deliberate, documented tightening: out-of-range values that are accepted today would become `422`. This must be recorded in the contract so no client is surprised.
* `[P]` `SourceOut` (`chat/routes.py:34-78`) gains `extractionMethod`, `figureRef`, `caption`, `elementId` — all nullable, so existing clients and legacy rows are unaffected, matching the class's established convention.

### 12.2 Status contract compatibility (REV-014; **the `partial`/frontend decision is now explicit — K-06/B-2**)

* `[V]` backend statuses: `processing`, `ready`, `error` (+ `uploaded` default, + proposed `partial`); the frontend union at `src/types/index.ts:8` is `'uploading' | 'processing' | 'ready' | 'error'` — it lists `'uploading'` (a **client-only** value set at `src/context/WorkspaceContext.tsx:142`) and **lacks `partial`**.
* `[V]` **the interim behaviour of an unknown value is not a rendering fallthrough — it is an unchecked cast.** `src/context/WorkspaceContext.tsx`'s `apiDocToFile` builds the UI model with `status: doc.status as DocumentFile['status']` (a `DocumentFile` field). A backend `partial` therefore passes the type check unchanged at runtime and reaches the renderer as a value the union does not describe, with no compile-time or runtime signal. R2 described this accurately but paired it with "Phase 1a forbids `src/**` edits", leaving the contradiction unowned (B-2).
* `[P]` **the eventual contract is unchanged and is not in dispute:** one coordinated change extending the union to the backend's real values (including `partial`), keeping `'uploading'` documented as client-side, and replacing the cast with an **exhaustive mapper** whose default branch produces an explicit `unknown` state the UI renders as such (never a silent fallthrough). A required test asserts that every backend value maps to a defined state and that an unknown value maps to the explicit unknown state.
* `[V]` `insights` already carries `status` (`documents/insights.py:205`), so both endpoints must stay consistent; a test asserts equality between the detail and insights statuses.

**The decision R2 left implicit (K-06/B-2) `[D]` — the owner must choose, and this plan does not choose for them.**

| | **Option A — coordinated minimum frontend change in Phase 1a** | **Option B — backend-only 1a, explicit compatibility window** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **What ships in 1a** | the backend verdict **plus** the three minimum frontend edits: add `'partial'` to the union (`src/types/index.ts`), replace the cast in `apiDocToFile` with an exhaustive mapper including the explicit `unknown` state, and render `partial` (with what is missing) plus the unknown state in `WorkspacePage.tsx` | the backend verdict **only**; `src/**` is untouched in 1a as Phase 1a's *Must not edit* list requires |
| **Ownership** | adds `FRONTEND_AGENT` to Phase 1a's responsible agents for the three files; the interfaces are `DocumentFile`/the mapper; `FRONTEND_AGENT` supplies the mapper test and `VERIFIER` reviews it. **No ownership contract is edited** — `src/**` is already `FRONTEND_AGENT`'s | unchanged (single owner, `BACKEND_AGENT`) |
| **User-visible behaviour during the window** | none — the UI understands `partial` from day one | **`partial` renders as the unknown value the union does not cover.** Because the cast is unchecked, the *observable* effect is whatever `WorkspacePage`'s existing status handling does with an unmatched value; the plan must not assert a specific rendering without reading that switch, and the honest statement is: **a `partial` document is not guaranteed any correct badge, label or "what is missing" text until Phase 6**, and could present as an unrecognised/generic state. The **backend** remains honest (status and, under gate 3/23, coverage numbers are correctly exposed); the **UI silently under-reports it** |
| **What gates it** | gate 4 (+ gate 12 for the frontend test-runner dependency if a runner must be introduced for the mapper test) | gate 4 only (+ gate 23 if coverage fields are reported) |
| **Follow-up required** | none for `partial` | **Phase 6 is mandatory, not optional**, for the union, the mapper and the rendering; the window's existence must be recorded in the Phase 1a completion evidence and in §19, and no acceptance criterion may claim the UI represents `partial` before Phase 6 |
| **Risk of choosing it** | a Phase 1a that is no longer backend-only: it introduces a frontend-owned file set and possibly a test-runner dependency (gate 12) into the first phase, against the plan's stated principle of keeping 1a dependency-free | a **knowingly degraded UI state** for the duration of the window, which is acceptable **only** if the owner accepts it explicitly; it is not acceptable to leave it implicit, which is what R2 did |

**Honest note on Option B's window.** The repository rule is *"Mirror real backend state. Never hardcode backend results."* (`agents/frontend-agent.md:23`) and `AGENTS.md` **§04 — Frontend** (which requires the frontend to "Represent loading, empty, error and unavailable states"). *(Attribution corrected after review: the prior draft also cited `AGENTS.md` §03, which is the general-rules section and does not contain that requirement.)* Option B does not violate those rules by hardcoding anything — the UI simply does not yet know a value — but it does mean the UI **under-represents** a real backend state for the length of the window. That is why the choice is the owner's and why the plan states it rather than assuming consent. **Neither option is selected in this document; both remain open under gate 4.**

### 12.3 Nonfunctional controls are forbidden (REV-011)

* `[P]` **No** `includeDiagram` field in the request contract and **no** diagram UI until the diagram capability exists (gate 9/10). The original plan's inclusion of the field with a note that it is "not a promise" is withdrawn: a field no backend can honour contradicts the frontend contract — *"Mirror real backend state. Never hardcode backend results."* (`agents/frontend-agent.md:23`) — and `AGENTS.md` §03. (**Attribution note:** the review's phrase "Do not add decorative controls" is not present in `agents/*.md`; the rule cited here is the one that exists.)
* `[V]` the **already-inert** `aiProcessing.depth` control exists today (`src/lib/api.ts:554`; `src/context/SettingsContext.tsx:29,45,60`; `src/pages/SettingsPage.tsx:32,281-288`) and is not consumed by the answer path. `[P]` Phase 6 must either **wire it to a real backend behaviour** (only if such behaviour is implemented) or **remove the affordance**; it must not stay inert. This is owner question 11 of the original plan and remains `[D]`.

### 12.4 Frontend rules carried forward

Reuse `src/lib/api.ts`; mirror real backend state; represent loading, empty, error, unavailable and unknown states; never hardcode backend results; never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; keep accessibility; do not add a dependency for convenience.

| first of {gate 3 option A, gate 3 option B, gate 26 (a)/(b)} | `008_*` | whichever DDL the owner approves first — the option-A `document_page_extractions` table, the option-B `documents.page_count` column, or C-3b's asset identity |
| second | `009_*` | the next approved change. **Intended mapping:** `008` = the gate-3 option (A *or* B, never both), `009` = C-3b's asset identity |

Only one of option A / option B can be selected, and gate 26's change is independent, so the worst case is two additive migrations (`008`, `009`), each with a real `downgrade()` that drops only what it created (§17.3).

### 13.2 Ownership corrections made by this revision (REV-009, REV-005)

* **`backend/config.py` → `BACKEND_AGENT`.** `[V]` `agents/backend-agent.md` Owns lists `backend/config.py`; `agents/ai-agent.md` lists `chat/**`, `embeddings/**`, `brain/**`, `graph/**`, `tokenization.py`, `outcomes.py` — **not** config. The generation work therefore *requests* a config field; it does not edit the file. Any config addition is a BACKEND_AGENT change applied once, sequentially.
* **`chat/routes.py` toucher list corrected.** `[V]` the original plan's note enumerated C-6/C-7/C-9 and omitted C-8, which also lists `chat/routes.py`. The revision states all four (C-6a, C-6b, C-7, C-8, C-9) as one `AI_AGENT`-owned, **sequential** series.
* **`documents/structure.py` added** to C-3 (and to any row that changes element content/type), because `[V]` it is the only writer of `document_structure_elements` / `document_table_rows` (`structure.py:79`; imported at `documents/routes.py:53`).
* **`documents/tables.py` recorded as the projection precedent** — the model a figure-asset or page-record projection should follow (pure, deterministic, database-free).
* **`backend/scripts/**` → `DATABASE_AGENT`** for the audit script `[V]` (`agents/database-agent.md`).

C-3b's model field is `DATABASE_AGENT`'s, but the **writer** is `BACKEND_AGENT`'s; the two land sequentially, and a gate-26 field must not be authored in the same step as a C-3 production edit |

(`C-15`, the audit script, is `DATABASE_AGENT`'s `backend/scripts/**` and does not touch this file) |

### 13.4 Minimal execution groups (REV-018, preserved)

Four writers — `BACKEND_AGENT`, `DATABASE_AGENT`, `AI_AGENT`, `FRONTEND_AGENT` — plus `TEST_AGENT` (tests) with `EVALUATION_AGENT` for research evaluation, `SECURITY_AGENT` auditing (remediation routed to the owning agent), and `VERIFIER` owning nothing and re-checking independently. **No agent is added to make the plan look comprehensive.**
