## 14. Phase Dependencies and Explicit Prerequisites (REV-008, REV-016)

### 14.1 Phase map

```
Phase 0  Reconnaissance ................ COMPLETE (original plan)
   │
   ├─ Phase 1a  Detection + honest status + honest reporting ......... NO new dependency, NO DDL
   │        │                                          gates 1, 4, 23, 24 (+12 if gate 4 selects
   │        │                                          frontend Option A — a mapper test runner)
   │        │
   │        │        (2a/2b hang off 1a — figures need no OCR and no schema change;
   │        │         a denied OCR gate 2/3 must not block figure work)
   │        ├─ Phase 2a  Figures + captions + run-scoped assets ... gates 5 (+26 for asset citation)
   │        │                          **+ 27 — PER-DOCUMENT RUN CLAIM AND CLEANUP EXCLUSION;
   │        │                          NOT APPROVED, SO PHASE 2a IS BLOCKED (K-01/K-02)**
   │        └─ Phase 2b  Equations ................... DEFERRED (gate 18)
   │                 │
   │                 └─ Phase 3b  Figure evidence in retrieval ....... gates 6 (+26 for asset citation)
   │                          │
   │                          ├─ Phase 5a  Diagram detection + existing-figure retrieval ... gate 9
   │                          │        └─ Phase 5b  Diagram generation/rendering ... gates 9, 10, 11, 22
   │                          │
   ├─ Phase 1b  OCR engine integration ........ gates 2, 3, 13, 16, 21, 22
   │        │
   │        └─ Phase 3a  OCR text in retrieval
   │                 │
   │                 └─ Phase 4   Mark-aware generation .... gates 7, 8, 21
   │
   └────────────────────────────────────────► Phase 6  Frontend .... gates 4, 12, 23, 26 (figures)
                                                     │
                                                     ▼
                                              Phase 7  Integration, evaluation, security, verification ... gates 14, 21
```

**Every gate in the map above is now identical to the gate list in that phase's §14.2 specification** (K-07/B-5 corrected five mismatches: gate **16** and **22** were missing from Phase 1b's map row; gate **21** was missing from Phase 7's; gate **26** was shown without its *conditional* status for 2a/3b/6; gate **22** was missing from Phase 5b's; gate **24** was missing from Phase 1a's, which also appeared as gates 1/4/23 in §5). Phase 1a's gate 12 is itself conditional on the gate-4 frontend choice (K-06).

**Hard edges:** 1a before 1b; **2a depends on 1a only for its data inputs** (figures need no OCR and no schema change — a denied OCR gate 2/3 must not block figure work) — but **2a also requires gate 27, which is a decision and not a dependency on another phase**, so 2a cannot start until the owner decides §10.6; 1b before 3a; 2a before 3b; 1a+3a before 4; 3b before 5a; 5a before 5b; 4 before 6 (for marks); 3b before 6 (figures); everything before 7. Research that changes no production file may run in parallel with 1a. **Phase 2a is the only phase whose start is blocked by a decision that no other phase can unblock** (gate 27), which is stated here so that the map cannot be read as "2a is ready once 1a lands".

**Phase 1a is deliberately first** because it is the only step that fixes the verified silent-loss defect **without** a dependency, a model, a migration or an owner schema decision.

### 14.2 Per-phase specification

Each phase records: objective · prerequisites · in/out of scope · responsible agent · files owned · interfaces · **files that must not be edited** · required tests · measurable acceptance criteria · security/data-integrity implications · **failure and recovery** · independent verification · completion evidence · approval gates.

**Phase 0 — Reconnaissance.** `[V]` complete (the original plan). No production change. Completion evidence: the original plan + the audit + the review report.
*Full field set:* Objective = establish the real architecture, confirm gaps, define owners/contracts/sequence/gates; Prerequisites = none; In scope = inspection and planning; Out of scope = any code change; Responsible = `COORDINATOR` (+ `VERIFIER` read-only); Files = none modified; Interfaces = none; Must not edit = everything production; Tests = none (reconnaissance); Acceptance = the original plan's §1-§13 delivered with verified evidence; Security = no secrets printed, no document contents enumerated; Failure/recovery = not applicable (no state changed); Independent verification = `VERIFIER` re-checked the claims; Completion evidence = the original plan, the audit and the review report; Gates = none.
*Note:* the same fourteen fields are given explicitly for every phase below (phases whose work is deferred carry them marked not-applicable rather than omitted).

**Phase 1a — Per-page completeness detection, honest status, honest reporting.**
*Objective:* every page is either extracted or explicitly reported as unaccounted, and no document claims to be complete when it is not.
*Prerequisites:* gate 1 (scope), gate 4 (`partial` contract + invariant restatement **+ the gate-4 sub-decision between frontend Option A and Option B, §12.2 — K-06**), gate 23 (coverage-field decision if any new field is reported), **gate 24 (the `MIN_USABLE_CHARS` value, its home, and the `error` reason set — the verdict cannot be computed without them; §14.2's `MIN_USABLE_CHARS` note below)**.
*In scope:* per-page decision inside the existing page loop; `ready`/`partial`/`error` verdict; the docstring/invariant restatement; consumer updates (§7.4); coverage reporting using only facts that can be told honestly.
*Out of scope:* OCR, any engine or dependency, figures, schema changes, per-page method/confidence claims.
*Responsible:* `BACKEND_AGENT`. *Files:* `documents/processing.py`, `documents/routes.py`, `documents/schemas.py`, `documents/insights.py`.
*Must not edit:* `chat/**`, `brain/**`, `graph/**`, `migrations/**`, `tests/**`, `src/**`, `requirements.txt`.
*Tests:* every transition (§7.6) and **every worked case of §7.2.2**, in particular: mixed PDF ⇒ `partial` while OCR is disabled; **scanned-only with OCR disabled ⇒ `error` / `no_text_layer_ocr_disabled` (never `partial`)**; a document whose every page lacks text ⇒ `error`; a raised step ⇒ `error` / `pipeline_exception`; no false `ready`; digital PDF unchanged (`tests/integration/documents/*`, `tests/unit/documents/test_phase3e_processing.py`).
*Acceptance:* 100 % of pages accounted for in the fixture set, **including a rotated-page fixture** (§15.2 — a rotated page that yields no text is an unaccounted page, so rotation is an accounting concern here, not only an OCR concern); `ready` never set with an unaccounted page; existing digital-format suites still pass; no change to chunk counts for digital PDFs; **reporting is status-only unless gate 23 selects option A or B** (no denominator is claimed otherwise — §8.2). **The verdict reason is transient in 1a (R2-04, §7.2.3):** it is returned in the processing result and written to the log; no Phase 1a test may assert that it is retrievable after the request completes or exposed through an API, unless a durable route was explicitly approved under gates 3/23.
*Security/data integrity:* no new input parsing; no new file access; no status written outside the pipeline.
*Failure/recovery:* a page-level failure degrades the verdict, never the process; a raised exception keeps `error` semantics; a reprocess replaces **database** rows **within one database transaction** — **and that word is the whole claim** (K-07/B-6): the transaction never covers the filesystem, never makes asset writes or cleanup atomic, and (from §10.5) is not the mechanism that makes replacement safe under concurrency. No Phase 1a statement may abbreviate this back to "replaces rows atomically" without the qualifier.
*Independent verification:* `VERIFIER` re-runs the fixtures and inspects the status transitions in the database.
*Completion evidence:* executed test output with exit codes, fixture results, the diff limited to the four files.
*Gates:* 1, 4, 23, **24** (+ **12** **only** if gate 4 selects frontend **Option A**, §12.2 — the mapper test needs a runner, which does not exist today).
*Full field set:* Interfaces = the existing extractor return shape + `Document.status` + `DocumentOut`/insights payloads; Must not edit = `chat/**`, `brain/**`, `graph/**`, `migrations/**`, `tests/**`, `src/**` — **see the gate-4 frontend note below: if the owner selects Option A, `src/types/index.ts`, `src/context/WorkspaceContext.tsx` and `src/pages/WorkspacePage.tsx` move from "must not edit" to "in scope for `FRONTEND_AGENT`, minimum change only"** — and `requirements.txt`.

**`MIN_USABLE_CHARS` — proposed home and owner (K-07/B-7). `[D]`** R2 named the constant as a verdict input but never said where it lives or who owns it, and gate 24 was written as if the value were the only open question. R3 records the three possibilities explicitly so the decision is answerable:

| Proposed home | Owner | Why it might be right | Why it might not |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **A module-level constant in `backend/documents/processing.py`** *(recommended, no config change)* | `BACKEND_AGENT` | the constant is used **only** at the point where the page's final text is measured, in the same module that already owns the extraction loop; it is **not** runtime configuration, so adding it to `config.py` would create a configuration surface nobody tunes. `[V]` `MAX_UPLOAD`-style limits that *are* operator-tunable live in `config.py`; a per-page content threshold that changes the verdict semantics is a **contract** value, not a deployment value | changing the constant changes document verdicts, so it needs the same review discipline as a contract |
| **`backend/config.py`** | `BACKEND_AGENT` (`[V]` that file is in its Owns list) | consistent with the other tunables, and it can be changed per deployment without a code edit | introduces a configuration knob whose value silently changes document statuses across a fleet; requires a `config.py` change, so it is **not** free (§13.1 C-1a's file list would gain a file) |
| **A named constant beside the verdict function**, exported for tests | `BACKEND_AGENT` | makes the value easy to pin in a unit test | adds an export surface for a value the parser/extractor do not need |

**Decision needed at gate 24:** the value, **and** which of the three homes above is chosen. **No new configuration value is introduced by this document** — the `config.py` option is listed as an alternative to be decided, not as a design. If the owner selects the `config.py` home, `backend/config.py` is added to Phase 1a's file list for `BACKEND_AGENT` in the same change, and that is recorded as a **gate-24 decision**, not an implicit addition.

**The `partial`-versus-frontend window — Phase 1a's scope depends on the gate-4 choice (K-06/B-2).** This phase's *Must not edit* list above forbids `src/**`, while §7.4 and §12.2 require the frontend union, the mapper and the rendering to change together with the backend value. R2 left the contradiction standing. The two admissible resolutions are §12.2's table:

* **Option A** — these three frontend files join Phase 1a's scope for `FRONTEND_AGENT` (the minimum change: `'partial'` in the union, the exhaustive mapper replacing the cast, and `partial`/unknown rendering). Phase 1a's completion evidence then also carries the mapper test output and (with gate 12) the runner dependency, and the phase is **no longer backend-only**.
* **Option B** — Phase 1a stays backend-only and **accepts a compatibility window** in which a `partial` document is not guaranteed correct UI representation until Phase 6. This is admissible **only** if the owner accepts it at gate 4; if accepted, the window is recorded in the completion evidence, the §19 deferred-capability row, and the Phase 6 prerequisites, and **no Phase 1a or Phase 6 acceptance criterion may claim the UI represents `partial` before the frontend change lands**.

**No Phase 1a acceptance criterion may assume either option.** The criteria below are written to hold under both.

**Phase 1b — OCR integration (gated).**
*Objective:* pages with insufficient native text are OCR'd when an approved engine is available, with provenance.
*Prerequisites:* gate 2 (engine + install + provisioning), gate 3 (option A/B/C), gate 13 (deployment change if the engine needs a system binary), gate 22 if deployment files must change.
*In scope:* one adapter interface (`documents/ocr.py`), per-page invocation condition based on real extracted characters, per-page records under the approved option, honest failure reasons.
*Out of scope:* changing the embedding model; OCR of pages with adequate text (a tested **negative** control); any hosted service without gate 16.
*Responsible:* `BACKEND_AGENT` (+ `DATABASE_AGENT` for option A).
*Tests:* scanned-only becomes searchable and is `ready` when every page succeeds; mixed PDF complete; adequate pages never sent to OCR (**negative control**); OCR failure on one page with usable pages elsewhere ⇒ `partial`; **OCR failure on every page ⇒ `error` / `ocr_failed_all_pages`**; **OCR output below the usability minimum ⇒ that page is unaccounted** (§15.1 row 31); **a per-page timeout or resource failure ⇒ that page is unaccounted, verdict per §7.2.1 (never `pipeline_exception`)** while **a raised/crashed runner ⇒ `error` / `pipeline_exception`** — both paths tested (R2-06b); retry replaces rather than appends (Option B, §10.5); rotated pages (§15.2).
*Acceptance:* per-page accounting preserved; every OCR page carries method + engine + confidence *if option A* (otherwise no such claim is made); measured per-page latency recorded; limits enforced.
*Security:* hostile-document bounds (page/pixel/time ceilings), no document content in logs, no egress without approval.
*Failure/recovery:* an engine failure is a **coverage** event whose verdict follows §7.2.1 — `partial` when at least one page remains usable, `error` when none does (C2); a crashed runner is reported, never retried into a loop; reprocess is idempotent.
*Verification:* `VERIFIER` + `SECURITY_AGENT` on the untrusted-input path.
*Gates:* 2, 3, 13, 16, **21** (the rate-limit value), 22.
*Full field set:* Files = `backend/documents/ocr.py` *(new)*, `backend/documents/processing.py`, `backend/documents/routes.py`, `backend/requirements.txt`, `backend/config.py` (BACKEND_AGENT), **`backend/main.py` (BACKEND_AGENT — the ask rate-limit middleware, matrix row C-1c, value = gate 21; `[V]` that file is in this agent's Owns list and already registers middleware)**, plus `models.py` + `migrations/versions/008_*.py` (DATABASE_AGENT, option A only) and `documents/page_extractions.py` *(new)* (BACKEND_AGENT); Interfaces = one adapter interface + the approved per-page record; Must not edit = `chat/**`, `brain/**`, `graph/**`, `src/**`, `tests/**`; Completion evidence = executed suite output with exit codes, per-page fixture measurements, the chosen option recorded, provisioning evidence (path + artifact only, never secrets).

**Phase 2a — Figures, captions, assets (gated; no DDL in-phase).**
*Objective:* figures survive extraction, persistence, storage and citation.
*Prerequisites:* Phase 1a; gate 5 (figure scope); **gate 27 — the per-document run claim and the active-run exclusion (K-01/K-02; NOT APPROVED, so this phase does not start)**; gate 22 only if assets require a deployment change (they do not — assets live in the upload volume). **`[V]` This phase's asset lifecycle is the only part of the plan whose correctness depends on a decision no other phase can supply**; §10.6 states the requirement and the eleven cases it must cover.
*In scope:* the **five** vocabulary/parser edits of §10.2 (items 1-5); **the chunk/embed exclusion (item 6, R2-01 — same phase, same owner; without it the caption is chunked twice and a caption-less figure is embedded as an empty chunk)**; image enumeration in place of the skip; caption association; **run-scoped** asset write under the §10.5 write contract; `figure` elements through the existing writer; **the replacement of the up-front derived-row delete with the Option-B ordering (write → commit → clean up) in `reprocess_document`** (§10.5, `[V]` `:696-705` is the current behaviour being replaced); **and, as part of that replacement, the claim and active-run exclusion Gate 27 selects** (K-01/K-02). **`[V]` The ordering change is a transaction-shape change, not a statement reorder:** today the derived-row delete and the replacement insert share one transaction (`:696-713`), so the delete must move to the post-commit sweep and the claim must commit before the work begins (§2.5 N-003/N-004, §10.6 point 10). This phase therefore has a **new** deliverable that R2 did not name — the run-claim mechanism — and it is blocked until Gate 27 decides its shape.
*Out of scope:* image understanding, OCR of figure text, generated figures, serving assets to anonymous clients, and any schema change (item 7 / gate 26 is **not** in this phase unless the owner approves it).
*Tests:* figure element emitted with page/bbox/caption; caption-less figure emitted with `content = ''`; **a caption-bearing figure produces exactly one ordinary chunk (the paragraph's) — no second caption chunk**; **a caption-less figure produces no chunk at all**; `DocumentChunk.element_type` never carries `'figure'` in the legacy or `structure-v2` path; asset written as a **regular file** with the server-fixed extension, and an existing destination name is refused rather than overwritten; **no row is written when the asset write fails** (persist-last, §10.5) and the failure is reported; asset deleted with the document, and on reprocess the new run-scoped set is committed before the previous set is swept; **failure injection** — asset-write failure, structure-persistence failure and a simulated crash before the commit each leave the previous committed structure **and its images** intact and citable, with candidate files reported as residue (§15.1 row 38); interrupted-run, orphan-asset, concurrent-run and missing-asset behaviour per §10.5; text extraction, caption chunk counts and table behaviour unchanged on a reference fixture; existing structure tests pass.
*Acceptance:* every detected image is accounted for (element + asset) or explicitly reported as failed; the caption appears in exactly one chunk; no empty chunk is created; no existing element type changes behaviour; **a failed replacement never leaves the document without its previous committed structure** (G1).
*Security:* containment, symlink/reparse refusal and exclusive creation reused for the new write helper; server-fixed format/extension (never from document content); explicit image `Content-Type` + `nosniff` on the serve path; no new path composition; tenant-scoped writes (R2-03).
*Failure/recovery:* a failed asset write does not fail the document and **persists no figure row**; it is reported (log counters + ingestion summary) and the image is uncitable — no failure reason is stored, because `[V]` no column exists for one (§10.5; durable provenance = gate 25). All twelve lifecycle states are specified in §10.5, **and states 1, 4, 5 and 12 were corrected in R3 (K-01/K-02/K-03):** a crash rolls the whole transaction back and leaves the **previous committed status, structure and assets** intact — it does **not** leave a stale `processing` row and is **not** recovered by the stale-run path; a concurrent second run is **not** prevented by the status guard; and cleanup **cannot** exclude an active run without the Gate 27 mechanism. **No failure statement in this phase may be restored to R2's wording.**
*Verification:* `VERIFIER` + `SECURITY_AGENT` (path/tenancy/write contract) + **a concurrency review of the claim and the exclusion before acceptance (§15.6/§15.7)**.
*Gates:* 5 (+ **26** only for enabling `figureRef`/asset serving) (+ **27 — required for the lifecycle itself, not approved**).
*Full field set:* Responsible = `BACKEND_AGENT`; Files = `backend/documents/figures.py` *(new)*, `backend/documents/processing.py`, `backend/documents/parser.py`, `backend/documents/routes.py`, `backend/documents/structure.py`, `backend/storage/file_store.py`; Interfaces = `ExtractedElement`, `ParsedElement`, the structure writer, the new write/read/serve/listing helpers; Must not edit = `chat/**`, `brain/**`, `graph/**`, `migrations/**`, `src/**`, `tests/**` (a gate-26 schema change is `DATABASE_AGENT`'s, in C-3b, not this phase's); Completion evidence = executed test output with exit codes, emitted-element and asset listings, caption-chunk counts on the reference fixture, failure-injection results, caption-association fixtures.

**Phase 2b — Equations — DEFERRED.** `[F]` Nothing is implemented; nothing is claimed (§10.3). Gate 18.
*Full field set:* Responsible = none (no phase runs); Files = none — `backend/documents/equations.py` is **withdrawn**; Interfaces = none; Must not edit = all production files stay untouched for this item; Tests = only the preservation assertion of §15.1 row 11; Acceptance = text is preserved verbatim or the page is reported; Completion evidence = not applicable while deferred; Gates = 18.

**Phase 3a — OCR text in retrieval.** *Objective:* OCR-derived text is retrieved like native text and labelled. *Prerequisites:* Phase 1b. *Responsible:* `AI_AGENT`. *Files:* `chat/rag.py`, `chat/routes.py`. *Tests:* a question answerable only from a scanned page retrieves it; `extractionMethod` present; abstention unchanged. *Acceptance:* correct document + page for OCR-only content; no fabricated similarity.
*Full field set:* Responsible = `AI_AGENT`; Files = `backend/chat/rag.py`, `backend/chat/routes.py`; Interfaces = `SourceChunk`/`SourceOut` (`extractionMethod`); Must not edit = `documents/**`, `graph/models.py`, migrations, `src/**`, `tests/**`; Security = OCR text is untrusted data (injection tests, §16.3); Failure/recovery = a missing or failed OCR record degrades to native-only evidence, never fabricated text; Verification = `VERIFIER`; Completion evidence = executed test output with exit codes and the retrieved fixture source (document + page); Gates = none beyond C-1b.

**Phase 3b — Figure evidence in retrieval (gated).** *Objective:* a figure question returns the right figure, document and page. *Prerequisites:* Phase 2a; gate 6. *Tests:* caption-mediated path; element-type-aware retention; explicit figure intent; caption-less figure (no invented caption); asset unavailable ⇒ honest failure — and, under the no-DDL fallback, the test asserts the **absence** of the asset route rather than a 404; cross-tenant asset request ⇒ 404 under gate 26; ranking reports no fabricated score. *Acceptance:* correct figure, document and page for a known fixture; zero fabricated captions; **exactly one chunk contains the caption text** — guaranteed by the chunk/embed exclusion in §10.1 step 6b (R2-01), not by hope; existing retrieval regressions pass. **Asset citation exists only under gate 26:** under the no-DDL fallback the figure is cited by caption and structure with an explicit "image not available in this configuration" state, and the asset route does not exist. *Verification:* `VERIFIER` + `SECURITY_AGENT` (IDOR).
*Full field set:* Responsible = `AI_AGENT` (retrieval) + `BACKEND_AGENT` (asset route and read/serve helper); Files = `backend/chat/rag.py`, `backend/chat/routes.py`, `backend/graph/service.py`, `backend/brain/adapters.py`, `backend/documents/routes.py`, `backend/storage/file_store.py`; Interfaces = Contract C fields + `figureRef` resolution; Must not edit = `documents/processing.py`, `parser.py`, migrations, `src/**`, `tests/**`; Security = IDOR/BOLA on the asset route (server-side ownership, 404), containment on read; Failure/recovery = unreadable asset ⇒ caption-only evidence + explicit unavailability; Completion evidence = executed test output with exit codes, fixture asset id/document/page, negative-control results (unrelated figure not substituted); Gates = 6 (+ **26** for asset citation / `figureRef`).

**Phase 4 — Mark-aware answers (gated).** *Objective:* genuinely different, source-grounded answers for 6/8/10/15 marks — or an honest unavailable state. *Prerequisites:* Phases 1a/1b/3a; gates 7, 8, 21. *Files:* `chat/answer_composer.py` *(new)*, `chat/routes.py`, `brain/adapters.py`; `config.py` by `BACKEND_AGENT`. *Must not edit:* the trained QA weights; the extractive limits. *Tests:* four structures measurably different by rubric; grounding; unsupported questions; insufficient evidence; model-unavailable fallback (§11.3); `marks=None` identical to today. *Acceptance:* ≥ the owner-approved thresholds per metric, or the feature stays disabled and the fallback is what ships. **Per-section evidence linkage is asserted** (`sectionEvidence`, §12.1). *Failure/recovery:* degrade to extractive with `degraded=true`; never fabricate.
*Full field set:* Responsible = `AI_AGENT` (+ `BACKEND_AGENT` for the `config.py` field); Files = `backend/chat/answer_composer.py` *(new)*, `backend/chat/routes.py`, `backend/brain/adapters.py`, `backend/config.py`, plus `backend/main.py` (owned by `BACKEND_AGENT` and delivered in Phase 1b as C-1c; listed here only so that the request limit and the generation budget are enforced by the same middleware); Interfaces = Contract D request/response; Must not edit = `backend/models/documind-qa/**` (weights), the extractive limits in `chat/qa_model.py`, migrations, `src/**`, `tests/**`; Security = no egress unless gate 16 approves it, instruction/data separation (§16.3), secrets env-only; Verification = `VERIFIER` + `EVALUATION_AGENT` metrics + `SECURITY_AGENT` (egress/injection); Completion evidence = executed test output with exit codes, the model decision record, measured metrics with the owner-approved thresholds named; Gates = 7, 8, 21; the ask rate limit it depends on is delivered by **C-1c** in Phase 1b (same limit value, gate 21).

**Phase 5a — Diagram detection + existing-figure retrieval (gated).** *Objective:* detect diagram need and return the right existing figure. *Prerequisites:* Phase 3b; gate 9. *Tests:* detection precision/recall on a fixture set; trigger recorded; correct figure or an explicit "no figure found" state. *Acceptance:* no unrelated figure substituted — including the **unrelated-but-similar-figure negative control** (§15.1 row 30); failures reported.
*Full field set:* Responsible = `AI_AGENT`; Files = `backend/brain/classifier.py`, `backend/brain/adapters.py`, `backend/chat/routes.py`; Interfaces = the detection signal + the 3b figure evidence; Must not edit = `documents/**`, migrations, `src/**`, `tests/**`; Security = retrieved figure must belong to the authenticated user's document (ownership re-checked); Failure/recovery = detection without a suitable figure yields an explicit "no figure found" state, never a substitute; Verification = `VERIFIER`; Completion evidence = executed test output with exit codes, detection precision/recall on the fixture set, the recorded trigger for each detection; Gates = 9.

**Phase 5b — Diagram generation/rendering (gated, optional).** *Objective:* generate and render only when justified, validated and safe. *Prerequisites:* Phase 5a; gates 9, 10, 11, 22. *Tests:* invalid markup rejected (fail closed); no script/remote fetch; rendering failure reported, never presented as success; attribution present; `includeDiagram`/UI only now (§12.3). *Acceptance:* the highest-risk capability ships only with the smallest-surface renderer approved.
*Full field set:* Responsible = `BACKEND_AGENT` (renderer, on `COORDINATOR`-approved ownership) + `AI_AGENT` (composition) + `FRONTEND_AGENT` (display); Files = `backend/diagrams/render.py` *(new package)*, `backend/main.py`, `backend/requirements.txt`, `backend/chat/routes.py`, `src/**`; Interfaces = Contract E (`DiagramPayload`) + the deferred `includeDiagram` field; Must not edit = untrusted-input-free paths only — never render arbitrary HTML/script; Security = XSS/injection/SSRF rejected and fail-closed, CSP, no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; Failure/recovery = validation or rendering failure is reported, never presented as success; Completion evidence = executed test output with exit codes, negative-fixture rejection results, the approved renderer decision; Gates = 9, 10, 11, 22.

**Phase 6 — Frontend integration (gated).** *Objective:* users see honest state. *Prerequisites:* Phases 1a (status), 2a/3b (figures), 4 (marks), gates 4, 12, 23. **K-06 note:** if gate 4 selects frontend **Option B** (§12.2), this phase is the **mandatory and sole** owner of the `partial` union, the exhaustive mapper and the `partial`/unknown rendering; that follow-up is not optional and the compatibility window opened in Phase 1a closes here. *Files:* `src/**`, `package.json`. *Tests:* status mapper exhaustiveness; `partial` rendering with what is missing; marks reach the backend; figures render through the asset route; unavailable states for marks/diagrams; a11y; build passes. *Acceptance:* no inert control remains (§12.3); no unsafe HTML; empty/loading/error/unavailable/unknown states all represented.
*Full field set:* Responsible = `FRONTEND_AGENT`; Files = `src/pages/WorkspacePage.tsx`, `src/context/WorkspaceContext.tsx`, `src/lib/api.ts`, `src/types/index.ts`, `src/pages/SettingsPage.tsx`, `package.json`; Interfaces = Contracts C/D/F as amended (including `sectionEvidence` and `partial`); Must not edit = `backend/**`, `.env`, `dist/**`; Security = user/document content and diagram output are untrusted (no raw HTML injection, mirror real backend state); Failure/recovery = each new state (unavailable, unknown, partial) is explicitly rendered rather than silently falling through; Verification = `VERIFIER` (+ `npm run build`); Completion evidence = build output with exit code, the status-mapper test results, a11y checks; Gates = 4, 12, 23, **26** (figures: asset citation needs gate 26; the caption/structure retrieval path does not).

**Phase 7 — Integration, evaluation, security, verification.** *Objective:* demonstrated end-to-end behaviour. *Prerequisites:* all phases; gate 14 for live E2E; gate 21 for metric thresholds. *Deliverables:* executed suites with recorded exit codes, evaluation report, independent security review, independent verification, documentation update. *Acceptance:* every capability carries observed status; anything unrunnable is reported **BLOCKED**, never as a pass; `[V]` integration tests self-skip or refuse without a database (`tests/fixtures/integration_db.py:294`, `tests/fixtures/disposable_db.py:36`) and `test_real_e2e_validation` errors by design — a skipped suite is never a pass.
*Full field set:* Responsible = `TEST_AGENT` + `EVALUATION_AGENT` (+ `SECURITY_AGENT` audit, `VERIFIER` independent); Files = `backend/tests/**`, `backend/pytest.ini`, `backend/evaluation/**`, `backend/architecture/**`; Interfaces = none (verification only); Must not edit = production source, migrations, `src/**`; Security = no destructive database test, no production data, no guard bypass; Failure/recovery = a failing check is fixed at its cause or reported, never suppressed; Completion evidence = executed command lines with exit codes, per-capability observed status, the blocked-item list, metric values with thresholds named; Gates = 14, 21.

### 14.3 Failure and recovery, per phase (REV-008)

For **every** phase: (a) a failed phase leaves the repository at the previous phase's state; nothing partially migrated and no document left `ready`; (b) application rollback = revert the code change (the derived data is rebuildable by reprocessing); (c) database recovery = forward-fix in preference to downgrade (§17.3); (d) a phase that cannot meet its acceptance criteria is **not** marked complete and its gate stays closed; (e) an incident record is written to `backend/architecture/**` by `COORDINATOR`.

## 15. Measurable Acceptance Criteria and Required Tests

### 15.1 Acceptance scope (each claimed test must be run later; **none is claimed to pass here**)

**Note added in R3 (K-01/K-02/K-03).** Rows **40-47** are **new in R3** and are **proposed criteria that no implementation can currently satisfy**: they are gated on Gate 27 (§10.6), and several of them are written so that they **fail against today's code** — that is intentional, because a criterion that the current status guard already satisfies would not test the property the plan needs. Rows **23, 24, 33, 34 and 39** were **corrected** in R3 (the changed cells are marked). The dedicated concurrency and cleanup criteria are §15.6 and §15.7; the orphan/residue criteria that replace "no orphan growth" are §15.5.

**Note added in R4 (`R4-08`).** Row **48** is **new in R4** and is likewise a **proposed criterion**: it addresses run-identifier collision behavior, which §10.6's `RI-6` requires to fail closed. Rows 1-47 are unchanged from R3 apart from the corrections R3 already recorded (23, 24, 33, 34, 39).

Residue is that run's own **unreferenced candidate files** (§10.5 state 5), prunable per file — and the **previous committed structure and its images remain intact and citable** (G1). **R2's cell said "status recovers through the existing stale-run path"; that assertion was wrong and is withdrawn.** For an interrupted **initial upload** there is no document row at all after the rollback and the stored file is an orphan upload |

Concurrent-run behaviour is asserted by **§15.6 (claim)** and **§15.7 (cleanup exclusion)** once gate 27 is decided |

Asserted only after gate 27 is implemented; the claim is verified to be **committed and visible to the other session before work begins** |
| **41** | **Two concurrent initial uploads of the same content hash (K-01/A-1)** | 1b/2a (gate 27) | one document row exists per (user, content) — or the plan must document the duplicate-row outcome it accepts. `[V]` today the dedupe probe (`:397-405`) is a snapshot read and `content_hash` has **no unique constraint**, so both requests can proceed; the criterion states which arbitration (claim, unique index, or explicit acceptance of duplicates) is required, and **does not claim it exists** |
| **42** | **A worker that exceeds the stale threshold while still running (K-01/K-03)** | 2a (gate 27) | the mechanism must not treat "600 s elapsed" as "the worker has stopped". `[V]` `_is_stale_processing` is exactly such a heuristic (`:66`, `:321-328`). The criterion requires an observable assertion that a **still-running** long OCR job keeps or can safely regain exclusivity, and that no second run commits over it |
| **43** | **An old worker attempts to commit after losing its claim (K-01)** | 2a (gate 27) | the old run's commit is **rejected or provably harmless**; the criterion requires the plan to state which, and the test to show that the committed structure afterwards belongs to the current claim holder. **This case was absent from R2 entirely** |
| **44** | **A crashed worker followed by a retry (K-03/A-3)** | 2a (gate 27) | the crash leaves the previous committed structure intact and (today) no claim behind; after a claim exists, the retry must acquire a claim that the crashed run cannot still hold, and the retry's outcome must be identical to any other successful run. The test must also show that **no recovery depends on the stale-run path being reachable** for the crash case |
| **45** | **A failed claim or a failed processing transaction (K-01)** | 2a (gate 27) | a claim that fails to commit leaves the document at its previous committed state with **no partial claim**; a failed processing transaction leaves the same, and in both cases no asset cleanup runs on the strength of the failed attempt |
| **46** | **Lease expiry while the older worker is still running (K-01/K-02)** | 2a (gate 27, if a lease mechanism is selected) | the criterion requires a defined, observable outcome: the older worker must not be able to delete or overwrite the newer run's state, and the newer run must not delete the older worker's still-needed candidate files. If a lease without this property is selected, the plan must record the accepted consequence |
| **47** | **Cleanup encounters a document whose active run state cannot be determined (K-02/A-2)** | 2a (gate 27) | **the sweep deletes nothing and reports** — the fail-safe class of §10.5's six classes. Asserted for the case where the run registry/claim store is unreadable, the run identifier is absent (e.g. an asset written before the mechanism existed) and the database is unreachable. This is the direct acceptance test for the A-2 defect |
| **48** | **Run-identifier collision or duplication (R4-08)** | 2a (gate 27) | a forced duplicate/colliding run identifier **fails closed**: the write is refused, **both** runs' files survive, the condition is **reported**, and no asset of either run is overwritten or deleted (`RI-6`). The criterion must also assert the two properties it depends on — that the identifier is **server-generated** and that a client cannot supply or influence it (`RI-1`) — and that the uniqueness expectation the mechanism documents (`RI-2`) is the one the test exercises. **No collision was generated in this pass**, and the test cannot be written before the mechanism exists |
| **49** | **Worker/process death mid-run (R5-01, `AC-9`)** | 2a (gate 27) | the **observable** outcome only: when the next run completes, exactly **one** authoritative committed structure exists, no committed element references a deleted asset, and the killed run's candidates are identifiable residue. **The claim-state clause is `DEFERRED`** — whether the dead run's claim is released, held until expiry, or reclaimable is asserted **only** once the mechanism exists. **A test that merely re-runs the crash+retry case does not satisfy this row** |
| **50** | **Container restart mid-run (R5-01, `AC-10`)** | 2a (gate 27) | the row-49 observable outcome **plus** two restart-specific assertions: the restart does not silently enable a second concurrent writer over the same document, and residue is **reported**. Claim-state clause `DEFERRED` |
| **51** | **Request timeout mid-run (R5-01, `AC-11`)** | 2a (gate 27) | a timeout leaves **no partially-committed structure** and hands no state to a competitor that would let two writers both commit. Release/expiry/reclaim clause `DEFERRED` |
| **52** | **Client disconnect mid-run (R5-01, `AC-12`)** | 2a (gate 27) | as row 51, **plus**: the disconnect alone must not be the trigger that permits a new run to proceed while the old one may still be writing |
| **53** | **Retry after interrupted execution without a crash (R5-01, `AC-13`)** | 2a (gate 27) | the retry produces the same derived rows as any successful run, never two live authoritative asset sets, and never leaves two structures that could both be considered authoritative (extends row 44's crash case) |

**Note on rows 49-53 (added in R5, `R5-01`).** They are **proposed and unauthorized**, like every other row in this table, and **their status is assigned only in §15.8** (SPECIFIED / PARTIAL / DEFERRED). **There is deliberately no row for termination/cancellation after claim acquisition**: that scenario has no criterion that can be written without selecting the mechanism, so it is preserved as an unresolved Gate 27 requirement in §15.8 (S5) rather than given an identifier that would imply a testable criterion exists.

### 15.2 Rotation and low-quality scans — measurable rules (REV-017)

`[P]` For each rotated/low-quality fixture the **only** acceptable outcomes are: (a) the page's text is recovered (rotation detected and applied or OCR succeeded) and the page is accounted for; or (b) the page is explicitly reported as unaccounted, with `rotation_applied`/`failure_reason` recorded where option A is in force. **Silent omission is a failure of the test.** Measurable acceptance: 100 % of pages accounted for (a count, not a quality threshold); accuracy thresholds for OCR text are **owner-approval items** (gate 21) because no baseline exists. If the owner prefers to defer rotation handling, that must be recorded as a deferral (gate 17) — not left implicit. **Phase placement:** rotation is asserted twice — as an **accounting** rule in Phase 1a (a rotated page that yields no text must be reported as unaccounted, never silently dropped; the **verdict then follows §7.2.1**, so a document whose every page is a rotated scan with no text is `error`, and one that still yields usable pages elsewhere is `partial`) and as a **handling** rule in Phase 1b (rotation detected and applied, or the page reported with its reason).

### 15.3 Metrics (separate per area; thresholds = gate 21, none invented here)

| Area | Metric | Method |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Extraction | per-page character coverage; OCR CER/WER | fixture ground truth, per engine candidate |
| Retrieval | Recall@K, MRR, path agreement, evidence coverage | existing `backend/evaluation/**` conventions |
| Generation | rubric structural completeness; accuracy vs evidence; redundancy | rubric per mark level |
| Grounding | unsupported-claim rate; per-section evidence linkage | claim→evidence inspection |
| Citations | document + page correctness | known-answer fixtures |
| Figures | detection precision/recall; retrieval correctness; asset-route rejection rates | fixture set |
| Diagram | invalid-source rejection; rendering failure reporting | negative fixtures |
| Reliability | p50/p95 latency per stage; failure/abstention rates; idempotency violations | instrumentation |

`[V]` evaluation is **not** pytest here: `backend/evaluation/**` is `EVALUATION_AGENT`-owned research launched explicitly (`agents/evaluation-agent.md`).

### 15.4 Atomicity and cleanup assertions — what may and may not be claimed (K-07/B-6)

**Rule.** Every use of "atomic", "atomically", "no orphan", "safe under concurrency", "never deletes" and equivalent phrasing in this plan, in any future phase report, and in any test name or docstring added under it, is bound by the following table. A statement that does not satisfy its row is a defect in the claim, not a nuance.

| Phrase | Permitted meaning | Explicitly NOT permitted | Where it legitimately applies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| "replaces rows atomically" / "atomically" about rows | **only** the database transaction (the writer's savepoint, or the request's single commit) | any implication that a **filesystem** write, asset write, asset deletion or cleanup is atomic with it | `documents/structure.py:103-117` (savepoint `:103`, `delete(DocumentTableRow)` `:110`, `delete(DocumentStructureElement)` `:113`, `flush()` `:117`; writer at `:79`); the single commit at `:456`/`:713` |
| "nothing is deleted before its replacement is committed" | the **ordering** property (G1), true for the single-run case | any implication that this makes cleanup concurrency-safe | §10.5 G1; §15.1 row 38 |
| "cleanup never deletes a referenced asset" | the file is not named by any element of the **currently committed** structure, **and** the file's run is provably not active | the bare predicate "unreferenced" treated as sufficient (that is exactly A-2) | §10.5 G3′ **with** G3″; §15.7 |
| "no orphan growth" | **not permitted as a criterion**. Replace with §15.5's measurable properties | — | nowhere; removed by K-04 |
| "safe under concurrency" / "the guard prevents a second run" | **not permitted at all** until gate 27 is implemented and its acceptance criteria pass | — | nowhere in this revision |
| "the transaction rolls back, so the previous state survives" | the DB transaction and the DB rows it covered | any claim about **files already written to disk** — those are not rolled back and remain as residue | §2.5 C-4/C-7; §7.5; §15.1 row 33 |
| "fail-safe" / "deletes nothing" | the classification could not be completed, so no deletion occurred **and the outcome was reported** | a silent no-op, or an unreported refusal | §10.5 cleanup procedure (vi); §15.1 row 47 |
| "run-scoped assets are disjoint" / "two runs can never share a name" (G2) | **conditional**: true only if the run identifiers themselves do not collide, and only once a run identifier exists at all | presenting G2 as a **current** property. No run identifier exists in the schema today, so G2 is a **required property of the gate-27 design**, not an observed fact | §10.5 G2, read with the `RI-2` uniqueness expectation; §10.6 `RI-1`…`RI-7`; §15.6 AC-8 |
| "the current run's residue" / "residue of run X" | **not used** to attribute residue to an unidentified run in the uncoordinated-overlap case. §15.7's overlap criteria are written as two explicitly introduced **test fixtures** (run A and run B) whose assertions are stated over *A's candidates* and *the winner's assets* (CC-1…CC-3), and §15.5's RC-3 and RC-5 state that they **cannot be evaluated until Gate 27's run registry exists** instead of naming a run | attributing residue to a run that cannot be named, or implying that one run can tell its own leftover files from another run's today | §15.5 RC-3/RC-5; §15.7 CC-1…CC-3; §2.5 N-005 |

**Run-identifier phrase rule (R4-08).** Every claim that run-scoped asset names are *disjoint* (G2) is **conditional on the run identifiers not colliding**, and must be written with that condition visible; the condition must name the uniqueness expectation that `RI-2` requires the owner to document. Because **no run identifier exists in the schema today** (`[V]` §2.5 N-005: zero occurrences of `run_id` and `asset_ref` in application code), any sentence about *this run's* or *another run's* files is a statement about the **proposed** design and must never be read as describing current behaviour. Resolving a collision by **reusing the name is forbidden** (`RI-6`): a colliding or duplicated identifier must **fail closed** — refuse the write, keep both runs' files, and report the condition.

**Why this is a section and not a footnote.** The R2 audit found two HIGH defects whose common root was a claim stronger than its mechanism (`A-1`, `A-2`). Both were *wording* defects in a document whose underlying engineering was mostly sound, which means the wording is the control. This table is the control.

### 15.5 Orphan and residue criteria — measurable replacements for "no orphan growth" (K-04/A-4)

`[P]` The following replace every "no orphan growth" requirement in this plan (§9.2, §16.2, §15.1 rows 23/34). Each is **checkable** against the artefacts the design actually produces, and each is consistent with a best-effort, approval-gated cleanup. **None of them promises zero orphans**; they promise bounded, identified, reconcilable residue.

| # | Criterion | How it is checked | Why it is honest |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RC-1 | After a **successful** run commits, exactly **one** authoritative committed structure exists for the document, and every figure element in it names an asset that the run wrote | structural read of `document_structure_elements` + element↔asset comparison after the run | this is the property users actually depend on; it does **not** require the filesystem to be empty of extra files |
| RC-2 | **No committed element references an asset that cleanup deleted.** The count of such references is **0** | before/after reference-set diff across a cleanup run, including a concurrent-writer test | this is the meaningful safety property, and it is exactly what A-2 violated once a second run exists — so its test must include a second run |
| RC-3 | **No asset belonging to an active or potentially-committing run is deleted** | §15.7's concurrency tests. **This criterion cannot be evaluated until Gate 27's run registry exists** (added after review): without a way to say "run A is active", the test suite cannot express the property at all, and must report it as **not covered** rather than as passing | the property the K-02 exclusion must supply; it is the concrete form of §10.6's requirement |
| RC-4 | A failed or interrupted cleanup leaves residue that is **identifiable**: the number of files it left, their document association, and the reason it stopped are **reported** | the cleanup run's report artefacts (counters + ids + reason), never silent | best-effort cleanup cannot guarantee completion, so it guarantees **reportability** |
| RC-5 | Residue is **bounded per document** relative to the runs that produced it (no unbounded accumulation per run, no growth independent of run count) | run count vs file count per document on a fixture with repeated reprocessing | keeps a real failure mode visible (leaks) without claiming perfection |
| RC-6 | Residue can be reconciled **only** through an explicitly authorised process, and that process is **read-only by default** and **aborts** rather than acting when its inputs cannot be read | the reconciliation tool's own dry-run/abort behaviour, matching `[V]` `backend/scripts/cleanup_orphan_uploads.py` | the owner keeps authority over deletion; the fail-safe precedent already exists in the repo |
| RC-7 | Cleanup failures are **observable and retryable**: a failure does not poison the document, and the next run or an approved sweep can complete the work | repeat the sweep after an injected failure; assert the document remains usable throughout | makes cleanup idempotent in practice rather than complete in theory |

**Owner authority preserved.** Which residue is tolerated, when reconciliation runs, and whether any automatic sweep is enabled at all remain the owner's call (§18). RC-1…RC-7 constrain *claims*, not policy.

**Dependency of these criteria on Gate 27 (added after review).** RC-2, RC-3 and RC-5 cannot be honestly evaluated under concurrency until the **Gate 27** claim/exclusion mechanism exists; RC-1, RC-4, RC-6 and RC-7 are evaluable for the single-run case today. A report that claims RC-2 or RC-3 without Gate 27 in force has not tested the property.

### 15.6 Per-document claim and concurrency acceptance criteria (K-01/A-1) — **proposed; not executed; blocked on gate 27**

`[P]` **None of these is claimed to have been run or to pass.** They are the criteria the owner's Gate-27 mechanism must satisfy before Phase 2a is accepted, and they must demonstrate the **invariant**, not merely exercise a status-check function:

| # | Scenario | The invariant it must demonstrate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AC-1 | **Two simultaneously issued reprocess requests** for the same document, arranged so both pass the status check before either commits | exactly one acquires the claim and proceeds; the other performs **no** row write, **no** asset write and **no** cleanup. **The test must fail against today's guard** (i.e. it must be constructed so a non-atomic check would let both through) — a test that passes on the current code proves nothing |
| AC-2 | **Two concurrent initial uploads of the same content hash** | a single authoritative document per (user, content), or the explicitly accepted outcome, asserted on the DB state, not on response codes |
| AC-3 | **A worker exceeding the stale threshold while still alive** (simulated long page) | exclusivity is not lost to a still-running worker; no second commit lands over it |
| AC-4 | **A worker crashes mid-run; a retry follows** | the crashed run holds nothing the retry must respect; the retry produces the same derived rows as any successful run; the document was never left in a state where two structures could be considered authoritative |
| AC-5 | **An older run attempts to commit after a newer run has taken the claim** | either the commit is rejected, or the plan states the accepted consequence and the test shows the committed structure belongs to the current holder — either way, **no committed structure references assets the winner deleted** |
| AC-6 | **The claim itself fails to commit**, and separately **the processing transaction fails** | the document remains at its previous committed state, no partial claim survives, and no cleanup runs as a result of the failed attempt |
| AC-7 | **Lease expiry while the older worker is still running** (only if a lease is selected) | the outcome is defined and observed: the older worker cannot damage the newer run's state and the newer run cannot delete the older worker's still-needed files |
| **AC-8** | **A duplicate or colliding run identifier is forced or simulated** (`R4-08`, `RI-6`) | the mechanism **fails closed**: the second run's write is refused, **both** runs' assets remain on disk, no asset is overwritten or deleted, and the collision is **reported** with the two identifiers. The criterion must also demonstrate that a **client cannot supply or influence** the identifier (`RI-1`) and must state which uniqueness expectation (`RI-2`) it relies on. **Writing this test requires the mechanism; it cannot be written against today's code, where no run identifier exists at all** |
| **AC-9** | **Worker/process death mid-run** (the process is killed; nothing is released) | the document's **observable** outcome is correct when the next run completes: exactly **one** authoritative committed structure exists, no committed element references a deleted asset, and the killed run's candidate assets are identifiable residue (`R5-01`, §10.6 property 9). **`DEFERRED` clause:** whether the dead run's claim is released, held until expiry, or reclaimable — and within what bound — is **not** asserted, because it depends on the mechanism the owner has not yet selected. **The criterion must demonstrate that the observable clause fails against a design that lets a second writer commit** |
| **AC-10** | **Container restart mid-run** (the process is replaced, not merely killed) | the same observable outcome as AC-9 **plus** two restart-specific assertions: the restart does **not** silently enable a second concurrent writer over the same document, and any residue it leaves is **reported** rather than assumed away (`R5-01`, §10.6 properties 4/9). **`DEFERRED` clause:** the claim's fate across the restart. A criterion that only re-tests AC-9 does **not** satisfy this row (that is the objection `R5-01` makes to "gap" wording) |
| **AC-11** | **Request timeout mid-run** (the request context ends while the work continues or is abandoned) | the timeout leaves **no partially-committed structure** and hands no state to a competing request that would let two writers both commit (`R5-01`, §10.6 property 4). **`DEFERRED` clause:** release vs expiry vs reclaim for the timed-out request |
| **AC-12** | **Client disconnect mid-run** | the same observable requirement as AC-11, with one added assertion specific to disconnects: **the disconnect alone must not be the trigger that permits a new run to proceed while the old one may still be writing** (`R5-01`, §10.6 property 4) |
| **AC-13** | **Retry after interrupted execution** (no crash — the run was interrupted, e.g. by a restart, timeout, disconnect or cancellation) | the retry produces the same derived rows as any successful run, never two live authoritative asset sets, and never leaves the document in a state where two structures could be considered authoritative (extends AC-4's crash case, which does not cover interruption without a crash) (`R5-01`). **Fully specified** |

**AC-1…AC-13 are the criteria. Scenario status is assigned only in §15.8.** Two required behaviours could not be turned into a criterion without choosing a mechanism, and R5 **does not** dress them up as covered: **termination/cancellation after claim acquisition** has **no criterion and no AC identifier** (assigning one would imply a testable criterion exists), and the **claim-state half of every scenario above** is `DEFERRED` to the mechanism proposal. Both are stated, with their statuses, in §15.8.


**Anti-patterns these criteria exist to forbid:** a "concurrency test" that only calls `_is_stale_processing`; a test that asserts a 409 without ensuring both requests reached the guard; a test whose assertions hold because the requests happened to be sequenced; and any test that passes identically before and after the claim mechanism is added.

**Required coverage of the eleven cases (§10.6):** every one of the eleven must map to at least one criterion, and **the mapping itself is a deliverable of the mechanism proposal** — it is not claimed to exist today. **The mapping, per case, is now stated in §15.8, which is the single source of truth for every scenario's status** (SPECIFIED / PARTIAL / DEFERRED); this section defines the criteria, §15.8 assigns their coverage, and it is **not** lawful to read a status from this paragraph. In outline: the failed-asset-write case is covered by **§15.1 row 38** ("Failed replacement preserves the previous committed structure (failure injection)", whose criterion injects "an asset write failing") — **not by any AC-row, which R4's corrected sentence had made ambiguous** (`R5-02`); the crash-and-retry case by **AC-4** and **AC-13**; the failed-claim/failed-transaction case by **§15.1 row 45**; and the cleanup-with-no-determinable-run case by **§15.1 row 47** and **CC-6**. **(History:** the pre-R4 sentence mis-routed "failed asset write" to row 45, which is the failed-claim/failed-transaction case; corrected in R4, and made explicit in R5.)

**Lifecycle-ending and environmental scenarios (added in R5, `R5-01`).** R4 recorded request timeout, client disconnect, container restart and unexpected termination as a **gap**. **A named gap is not a covered requirement, and it is not a criterion either** — so R5 supplies the criteria it can define without choosing a mechanism (**AC-9…AC-13**, below and in §15.8) and marks the rest **`DEFERRED`** with the requirement named. **The counts are stated explicitly in §15.8 and in this revision's front matter: 2 of 8 scenarios SPECIFIED, 5 PARTIAL, 1 DEFERRED with no criterion, and 0 recorded as fully covered.**

### 15.7 Active-run-safe cleanup acceptance criteria (K-02/A-2) — **proposed; not executed; blocked on gate 27**

`[P]` The purpose of every row is the same: **demonstrate that no committed structure is ever left referencing an asset another run deleted.** R2's design could not pass these, which is why they exist.

| # | Scenario | The invariant it must demonstrate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CC-1 | **Run A writes candidate assets while run B processes the same document** | B neither deletes nor overwrites A's candidates, and A's later commit is consistent with the files on disk |
| CC-2 | **Run B attempts cleanup while run A is active** | the sweep classifies A's files as active (or as unknown) and **deletes nothing of A's**; the outcome is reported |
| CC-3 | **Run A loses its claim before attempting to commit** | A's commit is rejected or provably harmless; no structure references A's now-superseded assets; the winner's assets are untouched |
| CC-4 | **A worker crashes after writing assets but before committing structure** | the previous committed structure and its assets remain citable; the crashed run's candidates are residue **and are not deleted while the run could still be active** |
| CC-5 | **A worker crashes after committing structure but before old-asset cleanup** | the new structure is authoritative and self-consistent; the previous run's files are identified residue; a later sweep is idempotent and resumable |
| CC-6 | **Cleanup encounters an asset whose run state is unknown** (unreadable registry, absent run identifier, unreachable database) | **nothing is deleted** and the refusal is reported — the fail-safe class (§10.5, §15.1 row 47) |
| CC-7 | **A retry encounters existing candidate assets from an earlier failed run** | they are classified correctly (failed-run candidates), and are deleted only when provably unreferenced **and** provably inactive; otherwise reported |
| CC-8 | **Overall assertion across CC-1…CC-7** | after every scenario, the diff of "assets named by committed structures" vs "files present on disk" shows **zero committed references to deleted files** (RC-2) |

**What must be true for these tests to be meaningful.** They require a way to make a run "active" observably (the Gate-27 mechanism), and they fail by construction against R2's design, where cleanup's only predicate was "unreferenced by the committed structure". A test suite that cannot express "run A is active" cannot test A-2 at all, and must be reported as not covering it rather than as passing.

### 15.8 Lifecycle-ending and environmental acceptance criteria — the Gate 27 scenario matrix (added in R5, `R5-01`)

`[P]` **Proposed; not executed; blocked on Gate 27.** This section is the **single source of truth** for the coverage status of every lifecycle-ending and environmental scenario. Status vocabulary, used consistently and never blurred:

| Status | Meaning | May it be called "covered"? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **SPECIFIED** | every assertion the plan can state **without selecting a mechanism** is written as a criterion, and the criterion is complete | **yes** — as a criterion; **not** as a passed test |
| **PARTIAL** | a criterion exists for the mechanism-independent clause, **and** the mechanism-dependent clause is explicitly `DEFERRED` with the requirement named | **no** — the scenario is **not** covered; it is partly specified and partly open |
| **DEFERRED** | **no criterion exists**; the requirement is preserved and blocked on the Gate 27 mechanism decision | **no** — an open requirement, explicitly not satisfied |

| # | Scenario | Protected invariant | Expected observable behaviour | Gate 27 requirement | Criterion | Status | Verification class |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **S1** | **Worker/process death during processing** | at most one authoritative committed structure; no committed reference to a deleted asset | the next run's completion leaves exactly one authoritative structure; the killed run's candidates are identified residue | property 9; `RI-3`/`RI-4` (run state and lifetime) | **AC-4** (crash + retry) + **AC-9** (observable outcome) | **PARTIAL** — claim-state clause `DEFERRED` | testable **after implementation**; impossible today (no mechanism, no live DB) |
| **S2** | **Container restart during processing** | as S1, plus: a restart must not silently create a second writer | as S1, plus the restart assertion and reported residue | property 9; property 4 | **AC-10** | **PARTIAL** — claim-state clause `DEFERRED` | testable after implementation; impossible today |
| **S3** | **Request timeout** | no partially-committed structure; no second writer enabled by the timeout | no partial commit; no state handed to a competitor | property 4 | **AC-11** | **PARTIAL** — release/expiry clause `DEFERRED` | testable after implementation; the *current* behaviour is documented in §7.5 and is **not** a claim |
| **S4** | **Client disconnect** | as S3, plus: the disconnect alone must not authorise a new writer | as S3, plus the disconnect-specific assertion | property 4 | **AC-12** | **PARTIAL** — release/expiry clause `DEFERRED` | testable after implementation |
| **S5** | **Termination/cancellation after claim acquisition** | a run that lost its right to commit must not produce an authoritative structure | **undefined today** — depends entirely on the mechanism (may a cancelled run commit? is its claim released? who may re-acquire?) | properties 4, 6 | **none — `DEFERRED`** | **DEFERRED** — **no criterion**; not covered | impossible today; criterion cannot be written without the mechanism |
| **S6** | **Correct acquisition/release/expiry/recovery when execution ends unexpectedly** | exactly one holder at a time; a dead or unknown holder is recoverable without asserting it has stopped | acquisition and recovery are observable; **release/expiry outcome for an unexpectedly ended run is not yet defined** | properties 4, 5, 6, 9 | **AC-3** (alive worker), **AC-6** (failed claim/transaction), **AC-7** (lease expiry, if a lease is selected) — **release/expiry clause: none** | **PARTIAL** — the release/expiry clause is `DEFERRED` | partly documented today (§7.5: a crash rolls back and leaves the previous state); the claim half is testable only after implementation |
| **S7** | **Retry after interrupted execution** | idempotent re-run; never two live authoritative asset sets | the retry reproduces the same derived rows; one authoritative structure | properties 5, 9 | **AC-4** + **AC-13** | **SPECIFIED** | testable after implementation; the single-run half is documented in §7.5/§16.2 |
| **S8** | **A competing request while a previous run may still be active** | exclusivity: the competitor must not write structure, assets or cleanup | exactly one run proceeds; the other performs **no** row write, **no** asset write, **no** cleanup | properties 1, 2, 8; `RI-1`/`RI-2` | **AC-1**, **AC-3**, **CC-2** | **SPECIFIED** | testable after implementation; **fails by construction against today's guard** |

**Coverage statement — exact, and deliberately unflattering.** Of the **eight** scenarios: **2 are `SPECIFIED`** (S7, S8), **5 are `PARTIAL`** (S1-S4, S6 — each has a criterion for its mechanism-independent clause and an explicitly `DEFERRED` mechanism clause), and **1 is `DEFERRED` with no criterion at all** (S5). **0 of the 8 are recorded as fully covered, and none becomes covered by being listed here.** Five criteria (**AC-9…AC-13**) and five §15.1 rows (**49-53**) were added by R5; **no existing criterion was weakened, re-scoped or counted twice** to make the matrix look complete.

**Scenarios and clauses with no criterion — `DEFERRED`, and explicitly not covered.**

* **S5 — termination/cancellation after claim acquisition.** Whether a cancelled run may commit, whether its claim is released, and who may then acquire it are all **mechanism-dependent**. **No criterion is assigned and no AC identifier exists for it**; the requirement survives as an unresolved **Gate 27** requirement (§10.6 properties 4 and 6). **Assigning an identifier would imply a testable criterion where none can be written.**
* **The claim-state half of every scenario above** (S1-S4, S6). The document/asset-state clauses are specified as criteria; the claim-state clauses are **`DEFERRED`** and must be supplied by the mechanism proposal, which §15.6/§10.6 already require to state the eleven-case mapping. **A `PARTIAL` row is not a covered row**, and this matrix must not be cited as evidence that these scenarios pass — or as evidence that concurrency is safe.

**What would change these statuses.** A `PARTIAL` or `DEFERRED` row becomes `SPECIFIED` only when the owner's Gate 27 decision selects a mechanism **and** the mechanism proposal states, for each of the eleven §10.6 cases, the asserted outcome; the criterion then becomes writable and testable. Until then these rows are **open requirements**, and a phase report that cites them as satisfied — or that cites this matrix as evidence that concurrency is safe — is citing something this plan does not claim.

**Source-criterion mapping used above.** The citation for today's behaviour in S3/S4/S6 is §7.5's transaction table and the source it cites (`documents/routes.py:445-461`, `:696-713`; `storage/database.py:56-63`), re-measured in R5 (`R5-03`). **No criterion in this matrix is sourced to a line range that was not re-measured in this revision.**

## 16. Security, Resource-Control, Idempotency and Failure-Recovery Requirements

### 16.1 Resource controls (REV-010) — values are owner decisions (gate 21)

`[V]` no rate limiter or inference limit exists today, and the deployment runs **one** uvicorn worker (`docker-entrypoint.sh:49`), so blocking work blocks the whole service. Required controls:

| Control | Requirement | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OCR page ceiling | max pages processed per document | `BACKEND_AGENT` | value: owner-approved, benchmarked |
| OCR per-page timeout | wall-clock ceiling per page | `BACKEND_AGENT` | must not be raised by a document; exceeding it is a **page-coverage failure**, not a pipeline exception (§7.2.1, R2-06b) — only a raised runner is `pipeline_exception` |
| Image/pixel ceiling | max pixels decoded per page | `BACKEND_AGENT` | hostile-document bound |
| Ingestion concurrency bound | bounded executor for OCR/figure work in `backend/documents/**` | `BACKEND_AGENT` | the event loop must not be blocked; async boundaries preserved |
| Composition concurrency bound | bounded generation concurrency + per-request token/time budget | `AI_AGENT` | applies only if gate 7 approves a generation model |
| Ask rate limit | requests per principal per window on `/api/chat/ask` | `BACKEND_AGENT` — middleware in **`backend/main.py`** (`[V]` that file is in its Owns list and already registers middleware); it must **not** require an edit to `backend/chat/**`, which is `AI_AGENT`-owned (`agents/backend-agent.md` Must Not Touch). **Scheduled as matrix row C-1c, delivered in Phase 1b/4, acceptance §15.1 row 22 (R2-05)** | limit **value**: owner-approved — gate 21 |
| Generation token/time budget | per-request cap | `AI_AGENT` | only relevant if gate 7 approves generation |
| Upload size validation | existing 50 MB cap reused (upload route) | `BACKEND_AGENT` | `[V]` `config.py:max_upload_size_mb = 50` |
| `topK` bounds validation | declared bounds on `AskRequest.topK` | `AI_AGENT` (`backend/chat/routes.py` is `AI_AGENT`-owned) | `[V]` `chat/routes.py:28` unvalidated today |

### 16.2 Idempotency and duplicate prevention (REV-010)

* `[V]` **today** reprocessing deletes derived rows first (`documents/routes.py:696-697` chunk loop; `:701-705` up-front `DocumentStructureElement` delete, statement on `:702`); `structure.py` replaces inside a savepoint; row evidence is unique on `(document_id, table_element_id, row_index)`. **Current behaviour only — superseded by §10.5's Option-B ordering (write → commit → clean up), R2-02.**
* `[P]` page records and figure assets must follow the same replace discipline; a second **sequential** identical reprocess must produce **identical derived rows and exactly one live asset set** (a required test — §15.1 rows 23 and 34; asset *filenames* differ by design under run-scoped identity, §10.5). **The words "with no orphan growth" are removed here (K-04/A-4):** cleanup is best-effort and reconciliation is approval-gated, so that phrase was a claim the mechanism cannot back. Its measurable replacements are §15.5's RC-1…RC-7.
* `[P]` figure assets follow the §10.5 lifecycle (**owner decision: Option B**): **run-scoped immutable names**, so a run never writes over a name the committed structure references; **write → commit → clean up**, so nothing is deleted before its replacement is committed; cleanup **scoped to the committed reference set *and* excluding every active or indeterminate run's files**, re-read immediately before each deletion, deleting only files it can prove both unreferenced **and** inactive — and, because a re-read cannot prove the second part on its own, deleting nothing whenever activity is unknown (K-02/A-2; §10.5's six classes; §15.7). Per-file deletion through the audited primitive; orphan reconciliation that reports before it deletes (approval-gated, dry-run by default, aborting rather than acting on unreadable inputs); no directory-walking deletion (the primitive refuses non-regular files by design, `[V]` `storage/file_store.py:188-192`); and **no filesystem/database atomicity claimed anywhere**. **R2's version of this bullet, which scoped cleanup to the committed reference set alone, is withdrawn: that predicate does not exclude an active run (§2.5 N-005).**
* `[P]` asset resolution derives its path from the **JWT-scoped** document row (never from a client-supplied id or path), so an asset can never be read across a document or tenant boundary — the ingest-keying rule itself is stated above.
* `[P]` a retried ingest must not duplicate chunks, elements, rows, pages or assets; ingest is keyed by document id, never by client-supplied identity.
* `[P]`**/`[D]` no bulk operation may be run twice concurrently on the same document — **and the guard does not deliver this** (K-01/A-1). `[V]` "status must not be `processing`" is evaluated on a snapshot read with no lock, no conditional update and no constraint, and the status it depends on is committed only at the end of the request, so two requests can both satisfy it (§2.5 C-1/C-2). The requirement therefore stands but its mechanism is **Gate 27**: an atomic, committed per-document claim (§10.6). Until Gate 27 is decided, **no bulk or reprocessing work may be described as safe against a concurrent duplicate**, and the operational mitigation is procedural (do not issue concurrent reprocesses) rather than technical. **R2's parenthetical here asserted the guard; it is withdrawn.**

### 16.3 Instruction/data separation (carried from the original plan, strengthened)

* Retrieved text is **data**. `[P]` the composition path must keep instructions structurally separate from evidence and must never place document text where the model can take it as an instruction; the model gains no tool, network or filesystem access.
* `[P]` required injection tests: a fixture document containing instruction-like text must not alter outcome classification, citations, ownership or tool behaviour.

### 16.4 Threat coverage (extends the original plan's §9, unchanged where it was already correct)

The original plan's thirteen-row security table remains valid and is now backed by these additions: the asset route is an IDOR/BOLA target resolved server-side with 404 for foreign documents `[P]`; path containment reuses `storage/file_store.py` `[V]` (no new ad-hoc path joining); diagram/answer output is never injected as HTML `[P]`; secrets stay env-only `[V]`; **no user document leaves the deployment** unless gate 16 authorises a specific service with UI disclosure `[D]`; no destructive database test is ever run against real data.

---

## 17. Deployment, Provisioning and Rollback Strategy (REV-015, N-002)

### 17.1 Provisioning order (mandatory)

`[V]` `docker-entrypoint.sh:19,29-31` refuses to start when a required model directory is absent (`require_dir`, `exit 1`), and models are read-only mounts. Therefore, for any newly required artifact:

1. Provision the artifact locally (operator action; agents may not write `backend/venv/**` or weight directories).
2. Mount it read-only at the expected path.
3. **Then** deploy the image that requires it.

Reverse order = a container that fails closed at boot. The fail-closed behaviour is **preserved deliberately** — it is a security property, not an inconvenience — and any change to it needs gate 13/22.

### 17.2 New-artifact decision

`[D]` For each new artifact (OCR model data, generation weights): is it **required** (fail closed) or **optional with degradation** (feature reports unavailable)? The revision recommends **optional-with-degradation for every new artifact** so a missing model degrades a feature instead of stopping the service — but this is the owner's call (gate 13).

### 17.3 Migration policy (REV-008)

* One linear head today `[V]` (`007_add_table_rows`); any new migration is additive-only with a real `downgrade()` that drops only what it created.
* **Forward-fix is the default.** A downgrade of a live database is permitted only under explicit owner authorization (gate 20) with a recorded backup step, because `AGENTS.md` forbids downgrading a live database without permission.
* Migrations are applied by the entrypoint before the API starts `[V]`; therefore a migration that fails must fail fast and visibly, never leaving a half-upgraded schema unreported.
* No destructive migration (drop column, drop table with data, type narrowing) may be authored as part of Phases 1a–6 without gate 3 and a separate risk note.

### 17.4 Deployment-file ownership (N-002)

`[D]` **Gate 22**: before any edit to `backend/docker-entrypoint.sh`, `backend/Dockerfile`, `compose.yaml` or `Dockerfile.frontend`, the owner must approve a `COORDINATOR`-owned amendment to `agents/**` naming the writer (recommended: `BACKEND_AGENT` for the backend entrypoint/image, `FRONTEND_AGENT` for the frontend image, `COORDINATOR` for `compose.yaml`). Until then these files are **frozen**.

## 18. Explicit Decisions Required from the User/Product Owner

**A recommendation is not authorization. None of the following is approved by this document.** Two of the gates below decide far more than the others and are the reason R3 was written: **gate 27** (Group F) decides whether the Option-B asset lifecycle can be built at all (§10.6), and **gate 4** now carries an explicit sub-decision between frontend **Option A** and **Option B** (§12.2, K-06). Neither has a default this document is entitled to assume.
